    readonly expectedType?: string | undefined;
    readonly actualType?: string | undefined;
    readonly issues?: readonly { readonly path: readonly (string | number)[]; readonly message: string; }[] | undefined;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

BCS content or a schema boundary did not decode.

`kind` says which of the three (`"type"`, `"bytes"`, `"shape"`) and is what a
consumer branches on; `issue` is the sentence for a human and is not stable.
It defaults to `"shape"` when neither a constructor nor an encoded value
carries one, so an extension that builds a `DecodeError` with no `kind` still
compiles and still answers the question conservatively.

### `DecodeKind` (const)

```ts
declare const DecodeKind: Schema
// decodes to: "type" | "bytes" | "shape"
```

Which of the three things that can go wrong at a decode boundary went wrong.

- `"type"`: the object, the field or the event **is not of the expected Move
  type**. The bytes were never parsed. This is the one a caller answers with
  "that is not one of mine" — a 404 for a foreign object, a `filter` over a
  heterogeneous list — and the one it is safe to swallow.
- `"bytes"`: the type matched and the **BCS parse failed**, or left trailing
  bytes. Either the layout this package was built with is not the layout the
  package on chain writes, or the object is corrupt. Never safe to swallow.
- `"shape"`: a **domain schema** refused a value that was already parsed or
  that came from the node as JSON — a missing field in a node response, a
  number that is not a timestamp, a simulation with no such command. A bug
  here is in this library, the node, or the caller's expectations.

Branch on this, never on `DecodeError`'s `issue`: `issue` is a human
sentence and its wording changes between releases.

### `DecodedDynamicField` (interface)

```ts
export interface DecodedDynamicField<S> extends DynamicField {
    readonly content: S;
}
```

`DynamicField` with its value decoded: what `getDynamicFieldOption`
answers when it is given a `schema`. `content` is the decoded value; the
raw bytes stay on `value.bcs`.

### `DescribeOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface DescribeOptions {
    /**
     * `false` drops the leading tag, for a caller that prints it in a column of
     * its own (`ExecutionFailed MoveAbort ...` becomes `MoveAbort ...`). Default
     * `true`.
     */
    readonly tag?: boolean;
}
```

What `describe` may be told.

### `Digest` (const)

```ts
declare const Digest: Schema
// decodes to: Digest
```

A base58 transaction digest. Not normalized — a digest has one spelling —
and rejected when it is not 32 bytes.

`Digest.make(value)` brands a value already known to be a digest (it
validates and throws); `Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(Digest)` is for one from
outside. There is no `normalize`, because there is nothing to normalize.

### `DynamicField` (const)

```ts
declare const DynamicField: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly type: string;
//     readonly $kind: "DynamicField" | "DynamicObject";
//     readonly value: {
//         readonly type: string;
//         readonly bcs: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     };
//     readonly version: Version;
//     readonly digest: string;
//     readonly name: {
//         readonly type: string;
//         readonly bcs: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     };
//     readonly fieldId: ObjectId;
//     readonly valueType: string;
//     readonly childId?: ObjectId | undefined;
// }
```

A dynamic field with its value. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.DynamicField`.

### `DynamicFieldEntry` (const)

```ts
declare const DynamicFieldEntry: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly type: string;
//     readonly $kind: "DynamicField" | "DynamicObject";
//     readonly name: {
//         readonly type: string;
//         readonly bcs: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     };
//     readonly fieldId: ObjectId;
//     readonly valueType: string;
//     readonly childId?: ObjectId | undefined;
// }
```

One entry of a dynamic-field listing. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.DynamicFieldEntry`.

### `DynamicFieldName` (const)

```ts
declare const DynamicFieldName: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly type: string; readonly bcs: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>; }
```

The BCS-encoded name of a dynamic field. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.DynamicFieldName`.

### `EXECUTE_INCLUDE` (const)

```ts
declare const EXECUTE_INCLUDE: {
    readonly effects: true;
    readonly events: true;
    readonly balanceChanges: true;
    readonly objectTypes: true;
}
```

The `include` set every execute, simulate and transaction read asks for, and
the one `Executed.fromTransactionResult` is typed over: a `SuiCore` read made
with `{ include: EXECUTE_INCLUDE }` is a result that method accepts.

### `Event` (const)

```ts
declare const Event: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly bcs: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>; readonly packageId: ObjectId; readonly module: string; readonly sender: SuiAddress; readonly eventType: string; readonly json?: unknown; }
```

An emitted Move event. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.Event` with the branded ids
this package uses.

The decode a caller wants is `SuiSchema.decode(codec, event.bcs)`, which
gives a typed value; `json` is the node's own rendering and is **not**
something to build on — it is absent on most transports and its shape follows
whatever the node feels like. It is kept only when the source carried it,
which in practice means a relay or sponsor envelope decoded through
`Executed.fromPartial`, where it may be the only form of the event there is.

### `EventEntry` (const)

```ts
declare const EventEntry: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly bcs: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     readonly packageId: ObjectId;
//     readonly module: string;
//     readonly sender: SuiAddress;
//     readonly eventType: string;
//     readonly checkpoint: bigint | null;
//     readonly transactionDigest: Digest;
//     readonly eventIndex: number;
//     readonly json?: unknown;
// }
```

An event read from a listing, with its place in the ledger. Mirrors
`SuiClientTypes.EventEntry`: an `Event` plus the checkpoint that
contains the emitting transaction (`null` where the transport does not say),
that transaction's digest, and the event's index in its list.

### `EventFilter` (type)

```ts
export type EventFilter = SuiClientTypes.EventFilter;
```

A filter on events: exactly one of `sender`, `emitModule` or `eventType`.
The SDK's `SuiClientTypes.EventFilter`, under its own name.

### `Executed` (class)

```ts
export declare class Executed extends Executed_base {
    readonly digest: Digest;
    readonly checkpoint: bigint | null;
    readonly effects: TransactionEffects;
    readonly events: ReadonlyArray<Event>;
    readonly balanceChanges: ReadonlyArray<BalanceChange>;
    readonly objectTypes: { readonly [x: string]: string; };
    readonly timestampMs: number | null;
    readonly sender?: SuiAddress | undefined;
    /**
     * Objects this transaction created, optionally filtered by Move type through
     * `typeMatches`: a bare tag matches every instantiation of the generic, a
     * parameterized one is compared in full after normalization. Never fails.
     */
    created(type?: string): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /**
     * Objects this transaction created whose reference satisfies a predicate.
     *
     * The direct replacement for the substring matching every repo hand-rolls
     * (`createdByType("::Receipt")`), without making substring matching the
     * default: `created(type)` still compares through `typeMatches`.
     * Never fails.
     */
    createdWhere(predicate: (ref: ChangedRef) => boolean): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /**
     * Objects this transaction mutated in place, optionally filtered by type.
     * An object that changed owner is mutated too, and is also in
     * `transferred`. Never fails.
     */
    mutated(type?: string): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /**
     * Objects this transaction deleted or wrapped. The refs carry the versions the
     * objects had going in, since they have no output version.
     *
     * Two effect shapes land here, because from a caller's side both mean "this
     * object is gone from where it was":
     *
     * - a **delete**, which the node reports as `idOperation: "Deleted"`;
     * - a **wrap**, which has no id operation at all (`"None"`) and shows up only
     *   as an input that existed and an output that does not. `wrapped`
     *   returns just those, for a caller that has to tell the two apart — a
     *   wrapped object still exists inside its wrapper and can come back.
     *
     * Never fails.
     */
    deleted(): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /**
     * Objects this transaction wrapped: they went in existing and came out
     * nonexistent without their id being deleted, which is how the SDK's effects
     * converter represents wrapping. They are a subset of `deleted`.
     * Never fails.
     */
    wrapped(): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /**
     * Objects whose **owner changed**, optionally filtered by type: they existed
     * going in, still exist with the same id, and their input owner and output
     * owner differ. Owners are compared by kind and party — an address, an
     * object (a dynamic object field's parent), shared, immutable, a consensus
     * address — never by version, so a transfer to someone else, a freeze, a
     * move under a dynamic object field and a move between `AddressOwner` and
     * `ConsensusAddressOwner` all count, while a consensus object whose
     * `startVersion` moved does not. A created object is not transferred (its
     * owner is on `created`'s ref), and neither is a deleted or wrapped
     * one. Either owner unknown — a reduced envelope that left it out, or the
     * `Unknown` variant — is not evidence of a change and is left out. Each ref
     * is the output side, with `inputOwner` the owner going in. Never fails.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    transferred(type?: string): ReadonlyArray<TransferredRef>;
    /**
     * Packages this transaction published (`PackageWrite` plus `Created`), as
     * full refs like every other accessor. A package's `type` is the literal
     * `package` when the node reports one, and the ref falls back to it when the
     * `objectTypes` join has no entry, so a publish is never silently dropped.
     * Never fails.
     */
    packagesPublished(): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /**
     * The net balance change for one address and coin type, in the coin's base
     * unit (MIST for SUI). `coinType` is any spelling — `"0x2::sui::SUI"`,
     * `CoinType.SUI` — compared under the one Move type rule. It covers coin
     * objects and the address balance alike, netted per address: a fold that
     * moves 1,000 SUI of coins into its owner's address balance reports only
     * the gas it cost (`Tx.foldMovement` reports the movement). Never fails.
     */
    balanceChange(address: SuiAddress, coinType: CoinType | string): bigint;
    /** Computation plus storage less the storage rebate, in MIST. Can be negative. Never fails. */
    get gasUsedTotal(): bigint;
    /**
     * An `Executed` from the SDK's own `TransactionResult`, read with
     * `EXECUTE_INCLUDE`.
     *
     * This is what `Tx.submit` uses, exported so a caller holding a result from
     * somewhere else — `client.core.executeTransaction`, a sponsor's SDK call —
     * can get the accessors without re-implementing the decode. `sender` is
     * filled when the result carries the transaction data (it was read with
     * `transaction: true` as well).
     *
     * Fails with: `ExecutionFailed` (the transaction applied and failed),
     * `DecodeError` (the response does not carry the include set).
     *
     * @since 0.1.2
     */
    static readonly fromTransactionResult: (result: SuiClientTypes.TransactionResult<typeof EXECUTE_INCLUDE>) => Effect.Effect<Executed, ExecutionFailed | DecodeError>;
    /**
     * An `Executed` from a **reduced** execute envelope: what a relay, a sponsor
     * or another service hands back, over JSON, after submitting on your behalf.
     *
     * Such an envelope is rarely the SDK's full include set. Everything optional
     * is filled in with "the node did not say" rather than refused:
     *
     * - `changedObjects` entries need only `objectId` and `idOperation`; the
     *   version, digest and owner on either side default to `null`, and
     *   `outputState` defaults to `ObjectWrite` (`DoesNotExist` for a
     *   `Deleted`), so `created` and `deleted` classify correctly
     *   from the id operation alone.
     * - `objectTypes` defaults to `{}`. **The type filters need it**:
     *   `created(type)`, `mutated(type)` and `expectCreated(type)` can only
     *   match a change whose type the envelope carried, so without
     *   `objectTypes` they return nothing. `created()` with no argument, and
     *   `createdWhere`, still list every created id.
     * - `balanceChanges` and `events` default to `[]`, `checkpoint` and
     *   `timestampMs` to `null`, `gasUsed` to zeros, and `effects.status` to
     *   success. `sender` (or `transaction.sender`) is kept when it is an
     *   address.
     * - **JSON spellings are accepted** where the SDK's types are not JSON:
     *   `bcs` as base64 or as an array of byte values as well as a
     *   `Uint8Array`, and every `u64` (versions, balances, gas, `checkpoint`)
     *   as a number or a `bigint` as well as the decimal string the wire uses.
     *
     * An envelope with no usable digest, or whose values are the wrong shape
     * rather than merely absent, fails: absence is filled in, nonsense is not.
     *
     * Fails with: `DecodeError`.
     *
     * @since 0.1.2
     */
    static readonly fromPartial: (envelope: unknown) => Effect.Effect<Executed, DecodeError>;
    /**
     * The single object of this type the transaction created.
     *
     * Fails with: `UnexpectedEffects` when the transaction created no object of
     * that type, or more than one.
     */
    expectCreated(type: string): Effect.Effect<ChangedRef, UnexpectedEffects>;
    /**
     * Every event this transaction emitted whose type matches the codec's Move
     * type, decoded with that codec, in emission order.
     *
     * The codec is a `SuiSchema.bcs(layout, type)` (or anything piped from one):
     * the type it was built with selects the events under the one Move type rule
     * (`typeMatches` — a bare tag matches every instantiation of a generic event),
     * and its layout decodes each one's `bcs`. Pass `eventType` to select by a
     * different type than the codec records, or to give one to a codec built
     * without a type.
     *
     * **After a package upgrade an event struct keeps the id of the package that
     * first defined it**, even when a later version emits it, so a codec tagged
     * with the latest package id selects nothing for a struct that existed in v1.
     *
     * No match is an empty array, not a failure; `expectEvent` is the
     * "exactly one" form. An event with no BCS (a relay envelope that rendered
     * events as JSON, read through `Executed.fromPartial`) fails to decode.
     *
     * Fails with: `DecodeError` — `kind: "bytes"` when a selected event's bytes do
     * not parse, `kind: "type"` when neither the codec nor `eventType` names a
     * Move type to select by.
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     *
     * @example
     * ```ts
     * import { bcs } from "@mysten/sui/bcs"
     * import { Effect } from "effect"
     * import { SuiSchema } from "@unconfirmed/sui-effect"
     * import type { Executed } from "@unconfirmed/sui-effect"
     *
     * const Posted = SuiSchema.bcs(bcs.struct("Posted", { index: bcs.u64() }), `${PKG}::board::Posted`)
     *
     * const indices = (executed: Executed) =>
     *   Effect.map(executed.eventsOf(Posted), (events) => events.map((event) => event.index))
     * ```
     */
    eventsOf<T>(codec: Schema.Codec<T, Uint8Array>, eventType?: string): Effect.Effect<ReadonlyArray<T>, DecodeError>;
    /**
     * The single event of the codec's type this transaction emitted, decoded.
     *
     * The "this call emits exactly one `Posted`" assertion, so a caller does not
     * write `events.find(...)` plus a decode plus a `die` for the missing case.
     * Selection and decoding are `eventsOf`'s.
     *
     * Fails with: `UnexpectedEffects` when the transaction emitted no event of
     * that type, or more than one — its `found` holds the emitting package id of
     * each matching event, so its length is the count; `DecodeError` when the one
     * event does not decode, or when there is no Move type to select by.
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     */
    expectEvent<T>(codec: Schema.Codec<T, Uint8Array>, eventType?: string): Effect.Effect<T, UnexpectedEffects | DecodeError>;
    /**
     * The package this transaction published — or the new version an upgrade
     * created — and its `UpgradeCap`.
     *
     * A publish creates exactly one package (`packagesPublished()`) and one
     * `0x2::package::UpgradeCap`; an upgrade creates the new package version
     * and **mutates** the cap it was authorized with, so `upgradeCap` is the
     * created cap when there is one and the mutated one otherwise. The cap is
     * found through `objectTypes`, which every execute and `Tx.submit` reads.
     *
     * Remember that a publish's `UpgradeCap` must go somewhere: the recipe
     * `transferObjects`es the `tx.publish(...)` result to the sender, or the
     * transaction fails with an unused value.
     *
     * Fails with: `UnexpectedEffects` when the transaction published no package
     * or more than one (`found` lists the package ids), or when no single
     * `UpgradeCap` was created or mutated (`found` lists the caps it saw).
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     *
     * @example
     * ```ts
     * import { Effect } from "effect"
     * import { Tx } from "@unconfirmed/sui-effect/tx"
     *
     * const deploy = Effect.gen(function*() {
     *   const executed = yield* Tx.run(publishRecipe, { signer })
     *   const { packageId, upgradeCap } = yield* executed.expectPublished()
     *   return { packageId, upgradeCapId: upgradeCap.id }
     * })
     * ```
     */
    expectPublished(): Effect.Effect<Published, UnexpectedEffects>;
}
```

A transaction the network executed, built from the fixed execute include set:
effects, events, balance changes and object types.

**`events` is `ReadonlyArray<Event>`, not `SuiClientTypes.Event[]`.** It
carries `packageId`, `module`, `sender`, `eventType` and `bcs`, with the
branded ids this package uses, and an **optional** `json`: the node's own
rendering, present only when the source carried one — a reduced envelope
read through `Executed.fromPartial` may; a gRPC execute never does. Do
not branch on it. The decode a caller wants is `Executed.eventsOf` /
`Executed.expectEvent` (or `SuiSchema.decode(codec, event.bcs)`), which
gives a typed value rather than a shape that changes with the node. Code
typed against the SDK's `Event[]` (whose `json` is required) therefore does
not accept these; take `ReadonlyArray<Event>` from `@unconfirmed/sui-effect`,
or map the fields you need.

The data fields are `digest`, `effects`, `events`, `balanceChanges`,
`objectTypes` (object id to Move type), `checkpoint` (`null` until the node
says), `timestampMs` (`null` likewise) and `sender` (0.5.0).

The accessors are **one implementation shared with `Simulation`**, so
a preview reads a simulation exactly the way a receipt reads an execution.

### `ExecutionFailed` (class)

```ts
export declare class ExecutionFailed extends ExecutionFailed_base {
    readonly digest: Digest;
    readonly effects: TransactionEffects;
    readonly reason: ExecutionReason;
    readonly command?: number | undefined;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The transaction was applied on chain and failed. Gas was charged.

### `ExecutionReason` (const)

```ts
declare const ExecutionReason: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly $kind: "MoveAbort";
//     readonly MoveAbort: {
//         readonly abortCode: bigint;
//         readonly location?: {
//             readonly function?: number | undefined;
//             readonly module?: string | undefined;
//             readonly package?: string | undefined;
//             readonly functionName?: string | undefined;
//             readonly instruction?: number | undefined;
//         } | undefined;
//         readonly cleverError?: {
//             readonly errorCode?: number | undefined;
//             readonly lineNumber?: number | undefined;
//             readonly constantName?: string | undefined;
//             readonly constantType?: string | undefined;
//             readonly value?: string | undefined;
//         } | undefined;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "SizeError";
//     readonly SizeError: {
//         readonly name: string;
//         readonly size: number;
//         readonly maxSize: number;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "CommandArgumentError";
//     readonly CommandArgumentError: {
//         readonly argument: number;
//         readonly name: string;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "TypeArgumentError";
//     readonly TypeArgumentError: {
//         readonly typeArgument: number;
//         readonly name: string;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "PackageUpgradeError";
//     readonly PackageUpgradeError: {
//         readonly name: string;
//         readonly digest?: string | undefined;
//         readonly packageId?: string | undefined;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "IndexError";
//     readonly IndexError: {
//         readonly index?: number | undefined;
//         readonly subresult?: number | undefined;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "CoinDenyListError";
//     readonly CoinDenyListError: {
//         readonly coinType: string;
//         readonly name: string;
//         readonly address?: string | undefined;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "CongestedObjects";
//     readonly CongestedObjects: {
//         readonly name: string;
//         readonly objects: readonly string[];
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "ObjectIdError";
//     readonly ObjectIdError: {
//         readonly objectId: string;
//         readonly name?: string | undefined;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "Unknown";
//     readonly message?: string | undefined;
//     readonly command?: number | undefined;
// }
```

Why a transaction failed on chain or in simulation. Mirrors
`SuiClientTypes.ExecutionError` variant for variant, including its `$kind`
discriminant, with two differences: `abortCode` is decoded to `bigint`, and
the shared `message` and `command` fields live on the error class that
carries the reason rather than being repeated in every variant.

The one exception is `Unknown`, which keeps `message` and `command`: it has
no payload of its own, and the node's sentence is the only place the real
kind (`InsufficientCoinBalance`, `FunctionNotFound`, ...) is written down.

### `ExecutionStatus` (const)

```ts
declare const ExecutionStatus: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly success: boolean; }
```

Whether a transaction succeeded. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.ExecutionStatus`
minus its `error` payload: the failure detail is carried by `ExecutionFailed`
and `SimulationFailed` as an `ExecutionReason`, so there is exactly one
representation of an on-chain failure.

### `ExtensionNotReady` (class)

```ts
export declare class ExtensionNotReady extends ExtensionNotReady_base {
    readonly extension: string;
    readonly member: string;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

A synchronous member of a Promise-faced extension was called before its
runtime existed.

`SuiExtension.fromService` builds its `ManagedRuntime` on first use, so until
something has been awaited there is no service object and no synchronous
member to read. Rather than hand back a Promise where the type says a value,
the face throws this. Two cures, both in the extension's own hands:
`await client.<name>.$ready()` once after registering, or register with
`warm`, which builds the runtime inside `register` and makes every member
real immediately.

Outcome `not_applied`: nothing was sent.

### `GasCostSummary` (const)

```ts
declare const GasCostSummary: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly computationCost: Mist; readonly storageCost: Mist; readonly storageRebate: Mist; readonly nonRefundableStorageFee: Mist; }
```

Gas cost breakdown. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.GasCostSummary`.

### `GasPaymentRef` (const)

```ts
declare const GasPaymentRef: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly objectId: ObjectId; readonly version: Version; readonly digest: string; }
```

One entry of a transaction's gas payment, as the bytes name it: the SDK's
own `{ objectId, version, digest }`. A coin reservation is one of these too
(version `0`); `BuiltGas.source` says which kind the payment is.

### `GasPaymentSource` (const)

```ts
declare const GasPaymentSource: Schema
// decodes to: "addressBalance" | "coinReservation" | "coins"
```

How built bytes pay for gas, as `Tx.build` reads it back out of them (and
as the span attribute `sui.gas_payment` names it):

- `"addressBalance"`: an empty payment; gas is drawn from the payer's SUI
  address balance at execution, and the bytes pin no gas coin.
- `"coinReservation"`: every payment ref is a **coin reservation** — gas
  drawn from the balance through a synthetic coin (version `0`), which is
  what a `tx.gas` user whose payer has a balance gets. It reserves the
  payer's whole balance as read at build time.
- `"coins"`: at least one ref is a coin object.

### `GetObjectError` (type)

```ts
export type GetObjectError = ObjectNotFound | ObjectDeleted | ObjectUnavailable | DecodeError | TransportError;
```

What can go wrong reading one object with a schema.

### `GraphQLUnavailable` (class)

```ts
export declare class GraphQLUnavailable extends GraphQLUnavailable_base {
    readonly method: string;
    readonly reason: string;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The GraphQL endpoint an extension needs is not usable: none was configured,
or the one that was could not be reached.

sui-effect does not wrap the GraphQL API — it owns the `SuiGraphQL`
*tag*, so two extensions that both read GraphQL share one client rather than
opening two. This is the failure the tag's `layerUnavailable` produces, which
is what an application provides when it has no endpoint: every call rejects
with this instead of the extension discovering a missing dependency at
construction. An extension maps it into its own union, or lets it through.

Outcome `not_applied`: a read that did not happen changed nothing.

### `HasOutcome` (interface)

```ts
export interface HasOutcome {
    readonly outcome: Outcome;
}
```

An extension error may declare its own `outcome`, and `SuiError.outcome` and
`Script.run` honour it before anything else.

An error that declares none and carries a tag the taxonomy does not own is
`"unknown"`, not `"not_applied"`: a tag this library has never heard of says
nothing about whether a transaction applied, and `"not_applied"` would tell
the documented retry idiom to send again on no evidence at all.

### `JournalError` (class)

```ts
export declare class JournalError extends JournalError_base {
    readonly cause: unknown;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The submission journal could not be read or written.

### `KNOWN_CHAIN_IDS` (const)

```ts
declare const KNOWN_CHAIN_IDS: Readonly<Record<string, string>>
```

The genesis checkpoint digest of each public network whose chain identifier
is fixed and known. These are the digests observed on the live networks
(`getChainIdentifier` returns the genesis checkpoint digest); the SDK ships
no such table. `devnet` and `localnet` are regenerated, so they are absent
and `Sui.layerNoDeps` records whatever the node reports instead of asserting.

### `KeyError` (class)

```ts
export declare class KeyError extends KeyError_base {
    readonly reason: KeyErrorReason;
    readonly message: string;
    /** Nothing was derived or sent. */
    readonly outcome: Outcome;
}
```

A recovery phrase, a secret key or a derivation path was refused. Nothing
was derived and nothing was sent: outcome `not_applied`.

`message` is a schema field because the sentence comes from the check that
failed; it never contains the phrase, a word of it, or the key.

### `KeyErrorReason` (const)

```ts
declare const KeyErrorReason: Schema
// decodes to: "unavailable" | "invalidMnemonic" | "invalidSecretKey" | "invalidPath" | "unsupportedScheme"
```

Why a phrase, a secret key or a path was refused.

- `invalidMnemonic`: not 12/15/18/21/24 words, a word outside the BIP-39
  English list, or a failed checksum. A mistyped phrase is always this,
  never a valid account nobody owns.
- `invalidSecretKey`: not a Bech32 `suiprivkey1…`, not 32 bytes (hex or
  raw), or not a valid key of its scheme.
- `invalidPath`: a derivation path that is not the scheme's Sui path shape,
  or an account index outside `[0, 2^31)`.
- `unsupportedScheme`: a scheme this constructor does not hold keys for
  (only `ED25519`, `Secp256k1` and `Secp256r1` derive from a mnemonic), or a
  `scheme` option that contradicts the key's own flag.
- `unavailable`: the platform has no `crypto.getRandomValues`, which a fresh
  mnemonic needs (React Native without a CSPRNG polyfill).

### `KnownNetwork` (const)

```ts
declare const KnownNetwork: Schema
// decodes to: "mainnet" | "testnet" | "devnet" | "localnet"
```

The four networks with a built-in default gRPC endpoint.

### `ListOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface ListOptions {
    readonly order?: ListOrder;
    /** Resume an ascending read strictly after this cursor; `null` is the start. */
    readonly after?: string | null;
    /** Resume a descending read strictly before this cursor; `null` is the newest. */
    readonly before?: string | null;
}
```

Where a ledger listing starts and which way it reads. The SDK's own
pagination options, under its own names: `after` resumes an ascending read
strictly after a cursor (a `ListPage.endCursor` you committed), `before` a
descending one; `before` implies `order: "descending"`. Give at most one.

### `ListOrder` (type)

```ts
export type ListOrder = "ascending" | "descending";
```

The ledger order a listing is read in. Mirrors the SDK's `order` option.

### `ListPage` (interface)

```ts
export interface ListPage<A> {
    readonly items: ReadonlyArray<A>;
    /** Where to resume: pass as `after` (or `before` when descending). `null` only before any position is known. */
    readonly endCursor: string | null;
    /** Whether the node said there was more after this page. */
    readonly hasNextPage: boolean;
}
```

One page of a ledger listing (`streamEventPages`, `streamTransactionPages`,
`tailEventPages`, `tailTransactionPages`): its items, and the cursor to
resume from once they are processed.

Commit `endCursor` **with** the items — in the same database transaction —
and a restarted reader that passes it back as `after` (`before` for a
descending read) neither skips nor repeats a page. Unlike the SDK's own
`endCursor`, it is never `null` once there is a position to resume from: an
empty page keeps the cursor it was read from, and a tail that has caught up
on a filtered listing moves it to the ledger head (see `tailEvents`).

### `Mist` (const)

```ts
declare const Mist: Schema
// decodes to: Mist
```

An amount in MIST. Encoded as the decimal string every SDK response uses.

### `MoveAbortInfo` (interface)

```ts
export interface MoveAbortInfo {
    readonly package?: string;
    readonly module?: string;
    readonly function?: string;
    readonly code: bigint;
    readonly abortCode: bigint;
    readonly constant?: string;
    readonly message?: string;
    readonly line?: number;
    readonly command?: number;
}
```

A Move abort, read off a `SimulationFailed` or an `ExecutionFailed` into the
fields a caller matches on.

- `module`, `function`, `package`: where it aborted, when the node said.
  **After an upgrade `package` is the id of the latest version** of the
  package, not the one the type was first published under, so match on
  `module` and `constant` (or `code`) rather than on `package`.
- `code`: the code as the Move source wrote it — `N` in `abort N` and
  `assert!(cond, N)`, and the declared `#[error(code = N)]` of a clever
  error. For a clever error with no declared code it is the raw
  `abortCode`, a packed integer: match on `constant` instead.
- `abortCode`: always the raw code the VM reported.
- `constant`, `message`, `line`: a clever error's constant name, its
  rendered value, and the source line of the abort.
- `command`: the index of the command that aborted.

### `MoveAbortMatch` (interface)

```ts
export interface MoveAbortMatch {
    readonly package?: string;
    readonly module?: string;
    readonly function?: string;
    readonly constant?: string;
    readonly code?: number | bigint;
}
```

What `isMoveAbort` matches on. Every field given must match; none given
matches every Move abort. `package` is compared as an address (padding and
case do not matter) — but see `MoveAbortInfo`: after an upgrade the node
names the **latest** package id, so prefer `module` plus `constant` or
`code`.

### `MoveLocation` (const)

```ts
declare const MoveLocation: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly function?: number | undefined;
//     readonly module?: string | undefined;
//     readonly package?: string | undefined;
//     readonly functionName?: string | undefined;
//     readonly instruction?: number | undefined;
// }
```

Where a Move abort happened. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.MoveLocation`.

`package` stays a plain string because the node may report a location for a
package that no longer parses as an object id.

### `Network` (const)

```ts
declare const Network: Schema
// decodes to: Network
```

The network a client is pointed at. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.Network`.

### `NetworkMismatch` (class)

```ts
export declare class NetworkMismatch extends NetworkMismatch_base {
    readonly expected: string;
    readonly actual: string;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The chain identifier the node reported is not the one the layer was built for.

### `Nonce` (const)

```ts
declare const Nonce: Schema
// decodes to: number
```

The replay-guard nonce a `ValidDuring` or `Validity` expiration carries. It
is a `u32` on the wire, so anything outside `[0, 2^32)` is a schema issue
rather than bytes the validator will reject later.

### `NotApplied` (class)

```ts
export declare class NotApplied extends NotApplied_base {
    readonly digest: Digest;
    readonly evidence: NotAppliedEvidence;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The transaction provably cannot have been applied, and never will be.

`evidence` is why. `"inputConsumed"` is only ever produced when a
**different** transaction's own effects report that it took a pinned object
at exactly the version these bytes pinned. An owned input that merely moved
on, one whose consumer took a later version, and one whose last mutation the
node will not name are all `SubmissionUnknown`, not this.

### `NotAppliedEvidence` (const)

```ts
declare const NotAppliedEvidence: Schema
// decodes to: "expired" | "inputConsumed" | "refused"
```

Why a transaction provably never applied, and never will.

`"expired"`: the expiration window was observed closed — the current epoch
past the recorded `maxEpoch`, or `chainTime` past a recorded `maxTimestamp`
by more than `SubmitConfig.expiryMargin` — around a `getTransaction` miss,
twice. `"inputConsumed"`: a **different** transaction's own effects report
that it took an object these bytes pinned at exactly the version they pinned.
`"refused"`: the node refused the first send outright (gRPC `INVALID_ARGUMENT`).

Shared by `NotApplied` and by the journal entry that records it, so the two
cannot drift.

### `ObjectDeleted` (class)

```ts
export declare class ObjectDeleted extends ObjectDeleted_base {
    readonly objectId: ObjectId;
    readonly version?: Version | undefined;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The object existed and has been deleted or wrapped.

### `ObjectEnvelope` (const)

```ts
declare const ObjectEnvelope: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly objectId: ObjectId;
//     readonly version: Version;
//     readonly digest: string;
//     readonly owner: {
//         readonly $kind: "AddressOwner";
//         readonly AddressOwner: SuiAddress;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ObjectOwner";
//         readonly ObjectOwner: ObjectId;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Shared";
//         readonly Shared: {
//             readonly initialSharedVersion: Version;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Immutable";
//         readonly Immutable: true;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ConsensusAddressOwner";
//         readonly ConsensusAddressOwner: {
//             readonly startVersion: Version;
//             readonly owner: SuiAddress;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Unknown";
//     };
//     readonly type: "package" | StructTag;
// }
```

The fixed set of object fields sui-effect always requests: `content`, plus
the owner, type, version and digest the SDK returns unconditionally.

### `ObjectId` (const)

```ts
declare const ObjectId: Schema
// decodes to: ObjectId
```

A 32-byte Sui object id in the padded lowercase `0x` form: the schema (which
pads on decode), plus `ObjectId.normalize` for a shorthand spelling such as
`"0x6"` and `ObjectId.Strict` for user input.

### `ObjectLookupError` (type)

```ts
export type ObjectLookupError = ObjectNotFound | ObjectDeleted | ObjectUnavailable | TransportError;
```

The failures of an object lookup.

### `ObjectNotFound` (class)

```ts
export declare class ObjectNotFound extends ObjectNotFound_base {
    readonly objectId: ObjectId;
    readonly version?: Version | undefined;
    /**
     * The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error.
     *
     * `Schema.TaggedError` gives every class the `Error` constructor and no
     * message of its own, so `error.message` was the empty string — and a
     * consumer that surfaces `.message` (a log line, a UI, another library's
     * error formatter) showed nothing at all. A getter rather than a schema
     * field, so it is always in step with `describe`, costs nothing to
     * construct, and stays out of `SuiError.toJson`'s encoding.
     */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The object does not exist, or has never existed.

### `ObjectRef` (const)

```ts
declare const ObjectRef: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly id: ObjectId;
//     readonly type: "package" | StructTag;
//     readonly version: Version;
//     readonly digest: string;
//     readonly owner: {
//         readonly $kind: "AddressOwner";
//         readonly AddressOwner: SuiAddress;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ObjectOwner";
//         readonly ObjectOwner: ObjectId;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Shared";
//         readonly Shared: {
//             readonly initialSharedVersion: Version;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Immutable";
//         readonly Immutable: true;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ConsensusAddressOwner";
//         readonly ConsensusAddressOwner: {
//             readonly startVersion: Version;
//             readonly owner: SuiAddress;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Unknown";
//     };
// }
```

Everything the transaction builder needs to consume an object again, plus the
type and owner so a caller can tell a shared object from an owned one.

### `ObjectType` (const)

```ts
declare const ObjectType: Schema
// decodes to: "package" | StructTag
```

The `type` field of an object envelope. Almost always a struct tag, but gRPC
reports the literal `package` for a Move package object (`grpc/core.mjs`
leaves `objectType` untouched when it has no `::`), and a package is a
readable object like any other.

### `ObjectUnavailable` (class)

```ts
export declare class ObjectUnavailable extends ObjectUnavailable_base {
    readonly objectId: ObjectId;
    readonly version?: Version | undefined;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

The node could not say what happened to the object (`ObjectError.reason: "unknown"`).

### `Outcome` (type)

```ts
export type Outcome = "applied" | "not_applied" | "unknown";
```

What a failure says about the transaction it came from, on the axis a caller
or a wrapper script acts on.

### `OutcomePhase` (type)

```ts
export type OutcomePhase = "pre-submit" | "post-submit";
```

Where in the lifecycle a failure was caught, which is the only thing that
can classify an error the taxonomy does not own.

`"post-submit"` (the default, and the 0.1.1 behaviour) is "bytes may have
gone out": a tag nobody here recognises proves nothing, so the answer is
`"unknown"`. `"pre-submit"` is a failure caught while **building, simulating
or signing** — an extension's own `PriceTooLow`, a validation error from the
caller's code — where nothing has been sent by construction and the honest
answer is `"not_applied"`.

### `Owner` (const)

```ts
declare const Owner: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly $kind: "AddressOwner";
//     readonly AddressOwner: SuiAddress;
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "ObjectOwner";
//     readonly ObjectOwner: ObjectId;
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "Shared";
//     readonly Shared: {
//         readonly initialSharedVersion: Version;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "Immutable";
//     readonly Immutable: true;
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "ConsensusAddressOwner";
//     readonly ConsensusAddressOwner: {
//         readonly startVersion: Version;
//         readonly owner: SuiAddress;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly $kind: "Unknown";
// }
```

An object owner. Mirrors `SuiClientTypes.ObjectOwner` exactly, including its
`$kind` discriminant, so `Owner.match` and the SDK's own narrowing agree.

### `PolicyDenied` (class)

```ts
export declare class PolicyDenied extends PolicyDenied_base {
  readonly rule: string;
  readonly message: string;
}
```

A preflight policy refused the transaction before it was signed.

### `Published` (interface)

```ts
export interface Published {
    readonly packageId: ObjectId;
    readonly package: ChangedRef;
    readonly upgradeCap: ChangedRef;
}
```

What `Executed.expectPublished` answers: the package id, the package's
own changed ref, and the `UpgradeCap` that governs it.

### `Recipe` (type)

```ts
export type Recipe = (tx: Transaction) => void;
```

A synchronous transaction draft. Reads happen before it, so it stays replayable.

### `SdkObjectRef` (interface)

```ts
export interface SdkObjectRef {
    readonly objectId: string;
    readonly version: string;
    readonly digest: string;
}
```

The SDK's own object reference shape, as `Transaction#objectRef` takes it.

### `ServiceInfo` (interface)

```ts
export interface ServiceInfo {
    /** The chain identifier (genesis checkpoint digest). */
    readonly chainId?: string;
    /** The chain's human name (`mainnet`, `testnet`, …). */
    readonly chain?: string;
    /** The current epoch. */
    readonly epoch?: bigint;
    /** The latest checkpoint the node has executed. */
    readonly checkpointHeight?: bigint;
    /** That checkpoint's timestamp, in milliseconds since the epoch. */
    readonly timestampMs?: number;
    /**
     * The oldest checkpoint whose data (transactions, events) the node still
     * serves. A pruning node moves this forward: a transaction older than it
     * answers `NOT_FOUND`, and a cursor older than it answers `OUT_OF_RANGE`.
     */
    readonly lowestAvailableCheckpoint?: bigint;
    /** The oldest checkpoint whose object versions the node still serves. */
    readonly lowestAvailableCheckpointObjects?: bigint;
    /** The node's software version. */
    readonly server?: string;
}
```

What a node says about itself: the chain, where its ledger head is, and — the
part a reconciler or an indexer needs — how far back its history reaches.
Mirrors gRPC `LedgerService.GetServiceInfo`; every field is optional because
the proto's are.

### `Signature` (const)

```ts
declare const Signature: Schema
// decodes to: Signature
```

A serialized signature, as every SDK signer returns it: the base64 of the
flag, signature and public key bytes. Branded so a signature cannot be passed
where a digest or an address is expected.

### `SignedTransaction` (const)

```ts
declare const SignedTransaction: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     readonly digest: Digest;
//     readonly sender: SuiAddress;
//     readonly signatures: readonly Signature[];
//     readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress | undefined;
//     readonly expiration?: {
//         readonly $kind: "None";
//         readonly None: true;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Epoch";
//         readonly Epoch: bigint;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ValidDuring";
//         readonly ValidDuring: {
//             readonly minEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly minTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly chain: string;
//             readonly nonce: number;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Validity";
//         readonly Validity: {
//             readonly allowedProposers: {
//                 readonly epoch: bigint;
//                 readonly proposers: readonly number[];
//             } | null;
//             readonly minEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly minTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly chain: string;
//             readonly nonce: number;
//         };
//     } | undefined;
//     readonly chain?: string | undefined;
// }
```

The signed bytes of a transaction, kept so an uncertain submission can be
reconciled or re-submitted later without rebuilding.

The expiration the transaction was built with rides along, because it is what
`Tx.reconcile` needs to decide that a transaction can no longer land.

`bytes` encodes as base64, not as a numeric object, so `SuiError.toJson` of a
`SubmissionUnknown` is JSON the operator who has to reconcile it can read.

### `SigningError` (class)

```ts
export declare class SigningError extends SigningError_base {
    readonly cause: unknown;
    readonly reason?: "expired" | "locked" | "rejected" | "unavailable" | undefined;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

### `SigningFailureReason` (const)

```ts
declare const SigningFailureReason: Schema
// decodes to: "expired" | "locked" | "rejected" | "unavailable"
```

Why a signer refused, when it knows: `"expired"` (a session-bound credential —
a zkLogin proof — whose `maxEpoch` has passed or would pass before the bytes
could land), `"locked"` (the secret was dropped by `lock` / `dispose` and must
be unlocked again), `"rejected"` (the person, or the wallet acting for them,
declined), `"unavailable"` (the device, wallet or remote signer could not be
reached). Absent for every other failure. A program branches on this, never
on `cause`.

### `Simulation` (class)

```ts
export declare class Simulation extends Simulation_base {
    readonly digest: Digest;
    readonly effects: TransactionEffects;
    readonly events: ReadonlyArray<Event>;
    readonly balanceChanges: ReadonlyArray<BalanceChange>;
    readonly objectTypes: { readonly [x: string]: string; };
    readonly commandResults: ReadonlyArray<CommandResult>;
    readonly gasPaymentMocked: boolean;
    /** `Executed.created`, over the simulated effects. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    created(type?: string): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /** `Executed.createdWhere`, over the simulated effects. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    createdWhere(predicate: (ref: ChangedRef) => boolean): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /** `Executed.mutated`, over the simulated effects. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    mutated(type?: string): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /** `Executed.deleted` (deleted or wrapped), over the simulated effects. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    deleted(): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /** `Executed.wrapped`, over the simulated effects. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    wrapped(): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /** `Executed.transferred`: objects whose owner changed. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    transferred(type?: string): ReadonlyArray<TransferredRef>;
    /** `Executed.packagesPublished`, over the simulated effects. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    packagesPublished(): ReadonlyArray<ChangedRef>;
    /** `Executed.balanceChange`: the net delta for one address and coin type. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    balanceChange(address: SuiAddress, coinType: CoinType | string): bigint;
    /** Computation plus storage less the storage rebate, in MIST. Can be negative. Never fails. @since 0.5.0 */
    get gasUsedTotal(): bigint;
    /**
     * `Executed.expectCreated`, over the simulated effects.
     *
     * Fails with: `UnexpectedEffects` when the simulation created no object of
     * that type, or more than one.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    expectCreated(type: string): Effect.Effect<ChangedRef, UnexpectedEffects>;
    /**
     * `Executed.eventsOf`, over the simulated events.
     *
     * Fails with: `DecodeError`.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    eventsOf<T>(codec: Schema.Codec<T, Uint8Array>, eventType?: string): Effect.Effect<ReadonlyArray<T>, DecodeError>;
    /**
     * `Executed.expectEvent`, over the simulated events.
     *
     * Fails with: `UnexpectedEffects`, `DecodeError`.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    expectEvent<T>(codec: Schema.Codec<T, Uint8Array>, eventType?: string): Effect.Effect<T, UnexpectedEffects | DecodeError>;
    /**
     * `Executed.expectPublished`, over the simulated effects: the package a
     * publish would create, and its `UpgradeCap`.
     *
     * Fails with: `UnexpectedEffects`.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    expectPublished(): Effect.Effect<Published, UnexpectedEffects>;
}
```

The result of a successful simulation, with the fixed include set `Sui` asks
for: effects, events, balance changes, object types and command results.

`gasPaymentMocked` mirrors the SDK field of the same name: `true` when the
simulated transaction had no gas payment, so the node ran it on a mocked gas
coin — gas costs and logic are exercised, but anything drawn from `tx.gas`
was funded by the mock, not the sender. `false` when gas was paid with real
coins or from the sender's address balance.

**A `Schema.Class` since 0.5.0** (a `Schema.Struct` before), so it carries
`Executed`'s accessors — `created`, `createdWhere`, `mutated`,
`deleted`, `wrapped`, `transferred`, `packagesPublished`, `balanceChange`,
`gasUsedTotal`, `expectCreated`, `eventsOf`, `expectEvent`,
`expectPublished` — through **the same implementation**, so a preview and
the receipt it predicts read the same way. The fields are unchanged. Values
come from `Sui.simulate` or a decode (`Schema.decodeUnknownEffect(Simulation)`);
one assembled by hand is `new Simulation({ ... })`.

### `SimulationFailed` (class)

```ts
export declare class SimulationFailed extends SimulationFailed_base {
  readonly message: string;
  readonly reason: ExecutionReason;
  readonly command?: number | undefined;
}
```

Simulation reported an execution failure. No gas was charged.

`message` is the SDK's own sentence; `command` is the index of the command
that failed, when the node named one — the same field `ExecutionFailed`
carries, so the two describe one abort the same way.

### `SimulationLookupError` (type)

```ts
export type SimulationLookupError = SimulationFailed | TransportError;
```

The failures of a simulation.

### `StructTag` (const)

```ts
declare const StructTag: Schema
// decodes to: StructTag
```

A fully qualified Move struct tag in normalized form: the schema, plus
`StructTag.normalize` for any spelling.

### `SubmissionUnknown` (class)

```ts
export declare class SubmissionUnknown extends SubmissionUnknown_base {
    readonly digest: Digest;
    readonly cause: unknown;
    readonly signed?: SignedTransaction;
    /** The one actionable line `SuiError.describe` produces for this error. */
    get message(): string;
}
```

Bytes may have reached the network and the outcome is unknown. Carries the
signed transaction so an operator or a later process can reconcile it.

### `Sui` (class)

```ts
export declare class Sui extends Sui_base {
    /**
     * Like `layerNoDeps`, but refuses to build when the node reports a
     * different chain identifier than `options.chainId`, whatever the network.
     *
     * Fails with: `NetworkMismatch`, `TransportError`.
     */
    static readonly layerNoDepsWith: (options: SuiLayerOptions) => Layer.Layer<Sui, NetworkMismatch | TransportError, SuiCore>;
    /**
     * `Sui` over whatever `SuiCore` is provided, **without reading the chain
     * identifier**: the one given is taken as the chain's.
     *
     * This exists for one caller: `SuiExtension.fromService` with `warm`, which
     * builds its runtime synchronously inside `register` and therefore cannot
     * await a `getChainIdentifier` round trip. Nothing is asserted, because
     * nothing is asked — a node on another chain is not detected here. What still
     * protects a warm registration is the chain itself: `Tx.build` stamps this id
     * on the transaction's `ValidDuring` expiration, and a validator refuses
     * bytes signed for another chain.
     *
     * Prefer `layerNoDeps`, which asks. Never fails.
     */
    static readonly layerNoDepsPinned: (chainId: string) => Layer.Layer<Sui, never, SuiCore>;
    /**
     * Builds `Sui` over whatever `SuiCore` is provided, reading the chain
     * identifier once.
     *
     * When the client's network is one of the entries in `KNOWN_CHAIN_IDS`
     * (`mainnet`, `testnet`) the identifier the node reports must match it, so a
     * program pointed at the wrong node fails at layer build rather than reading
     * the wrong chain. `devnet`, `localnet` and custom networks have no fixed
     * identifier: the observed one is recorded on `Sui.chainId` and nothing is
     * asserted unless `layerNoDepsWith` is given a `chainId`.
     *
     * Fails with: `NetworkMismatch`, `TransportError`.
     */
    static readonly layerNoDeps: Layer.Layer<Sui, NetworkMismatch | TransportError, SuiCore>;
    /**
     * `layerNoDeps` over a gRPC `SuiCore`.
     *
     * Fails with: `NetworkMismatch`, `TransportError`.
     */
    static readonly layer: (options: SuiGrpcLayerOptions & SuiLayerOptions) => Layer.Layer<Sui, NetworkMismatch | TransportError>;
    /**
     * `layerNoDeps` over `SuiCore.layerConfig`.
     *
     * Fails with: `ConfigError`, `NetworkMismatch`, `TransportError`.
     */
    static readonly layerConfig: Layer.Layer<Sui, Config.ConfigError | NetworkMismatch | TransportError>;
}
```

The opinionated tier.

### `SuiAddress` (const)

```ts
declare const SuiAddress: Schema
// decodes to: SuiAddress
```

A 32-byte Sui account address in the padded lowercase `0x` form: the schema
(which pads on decode), plus `SuiAddress.normalize` for a shorthand spelling
such as `"0x2"` and `SuiAddress.Strict` for user input.

### `SuiCore` (class)

```ts
export declare class SuiCore extends SuiCore_base {
    /**
     * Wraps a client the caller already built and configured; `options` sets the
     * read tier's request timeout and retry schedule. Never fails.
     */
    static readonly layerFromClient: (client: ClientWithCoreApi, options?: SuiCoreReadOptions) => Layer.Layer<SuiCore>;
    /** Builds a `SuiGrpcClient` and wraps it. Never fails. */
    static readonly layerGrpc: (options: SuiGrpcLayerOptions) => Layer.Layer<SuiCore>;
    /**
     * Reads `SUI_NETWORK` (required, no default), `SUI_RPC_URL` (optional,
     * defaulted from `defaultGrpcUrl` for the four known networks) and
     * `SUI_RPC_TIMEOUT` (optional, a duration such as `30 seconds` or
     * `Infinity`; the per-attempt `requestTimeout`, thirty seconds when unset).
     *
     * Fails with: `ConfigError` (the layer) when `SUI_NETWORK` is missing, when
     * it names a network with no built-in URL and `SUI_RPC_URL` is not set, or
     * when `SUI_RPC_TIMEOUT` is not a duration.
     */
    static readonly layerConfig: Layer.Layer<SuiCore, Config.ConfigError>;
}
```

The mechanical tier over `@mysten/sui`.

### `SuiCoreError` (type)

```ts
export type SuiCoreError = TransportError | ObjectNotFound | ObjectDeleted | ObjectUnavailable | TransactionNotFound | SimulationFailed;
```

Every failure `mapSdkError` can produce.

### `SuiCoreReadOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface SuiCoreReadOptions {
    /**
     * How long **one attempt** of a read may take before it is abandoned — its
     * request cancelled through the `AbortSignal` — and counted as a retryable
     * `TransportError { status: "DEADLINE_EXCEEDED" }`, which the retry policy
     * then retries like any other. Defaults to thirty seconds; `"Infinity"`
     * turns it off.
     *
     * This is what stops a node that accepts the connection and never answers
     * from hanging a program forever, the layer's own `getChainIdentifier`
     * included. It is not the gRPC client's `timeout`, which only sets a
     * `grpc-timeout` header the server may ignore. It does not apply to
     * `executeTransaction` and `signAndExecuteTransaction` (`Tx.submit` has its
     * own `SubmitConfig.executeTimeout`), `waitForTransaction` (which takes its
     * own `timeout`) or `use`.
     */
    readonly requestTimeout?: Duration.Input;
    /**
     * The schedule a read is retried on, replacing the default (jittered
     * exponential from 250 ms, capped at 10 s, five attempts in all). It sees
     * every failure but is only consulted while the failure is a retryable
     * `TransportError`; it decides the delays and when to stop, so give it a
     * limit (`Schedule.recurs`, `Schedule.during`) unless retrying forever is
     * meant.
     */
    readonly readRetry?: Schedule.Schedule<unknown, unknown>;
}
```

How the read tier behaves: the per-attempt request timeout and the retry
schedule. Every field is optional; the defaults are the spec's.

### `SuiCoreService` (interface)

```ts
export interface SuiCoreService {
    /** The network the underlying client was constructed for. */
    readonly network: SuiClientTypes.Network;
    /** Reads a batch of objects. Per-item failures are `Error` values in the result. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getObjects: <Include extends ObjectInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.GetObjectsOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetObjectsResponse<Include>, TransportError>;
    /** Reads one object. Fails with: `ObjectNotFound`, `ObjectDeleted`, `ObjectUnavailable`, `TransportError`. */
    readonly getObject: <Include extends ObjectInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.GetObjectOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetObjectResponse<Include>, ObjectLookupError>;
    /** One page of the objects an address owns. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly listOwnedObjects: <Include extends ObjectInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.ListOwnedObjectsOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ListOwnedObjectsResponse<Include>, TransportError>;
    /** One page of the coins an address owns. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly listCoins: (options: SuiClientTypes.ListCoinsOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ListCoinsResponse, TransportError>;
    /** One page of a parent object's dynamic fields. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly listDynamicFields: (options: SuiClientTypes.ListDynamicFieldsOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ListDynamicFieldsResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Reads one dynamic field. Fails with: `ObjectNotFound`, `ObjectDeleted`, `ObjectUnavailable`, `TransportError`. */
    readonly getDynamicField: (options: SuiClientTypes.GetDynamicFieldOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetDynamicFieldResponse, ObjectLookupError>;
    /** Reads one dynamic object field. Fails with: `ObjectNotFound`, `ObjectDeleted`, `ObjectUnavailable`, `TransportError`. */
    readonly getDynamicObjectField: <Include extends ObjectInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.GetDynamicObjectFieldOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetDynamicObjectFieldResponse<Include>, ObjectLookupError>;
    /** The balance of one coin type for one owner. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getBalance: (options: SuiClientTypes.GetBalanceOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetBalanceResponse, TransportError>;
    /** One page of every balance an owner holds. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly listBalances: (options: SuiClientTypes.ListBalancesOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ListBalancesResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Coin metadata for a coin type. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getCoinMetadata: (options: SuiClientTypes.GetCoinMetadataOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetCoinMetadataResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Reads an executed transaction by digest. Fails with: `TransactionNotFound`, `TransportError`. */
    readonly getTransaction: <Include extends TransactionInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.GetTransactionOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.TransactionResult<Include>, TransactionLookupError>;
    /** Submits signed bytes. Never retried at this tier. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly executeTransaction: <Include extends TransactionInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.ExecuteTransactionOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.TransactionResult<Include>, TransportError>;
    /** Signs and submits in one call. Never retried at this tier. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly signAndExecuteTransaction: <Include extends TransactionInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.SignAndExecuteTransactionOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.TransactionResult<Include>, TransportError>;
    /**
     * Reads one object **at a specific version**, for the one question the
     * live-object read cannot answer: which transaction consumed the version a
     * set of bytes pinned.
     *
     * `previousTransaction` on the *current* object names the latest mutation,
     * which is not necessarily the consumer of an older version: T can consume
     * version 3 and U version 4, and the live object then names U. Sui assigns
     * outputs the transaction's Lamport version, so the consumer of version `v`
     * is *not* generally the object at `v + 1`; `Tx.reconcile` identifies it by
     * walking the live object's `previousTransaction` and its effects'
     * `inputVersion` instead. This read stays public as a primitive for callers
     * that know a specific historical version exists.
     *
     * The SDK's `GetObjectOptions` carries no version, so this is not a wrap of a
     * Core method: it reaches the transport's own historical read — the gRPC
     * `LedgerService.GetObject` with a `version`, or JSON-RPC
     * `sui_tryGetPastObject` — and answers `Absent` on any transport that has
     * neither, on a version that was pruned, and on an object that never had it.
     * `Absent` is never evidence of anything; a caller that needs proof treats it
     * as "unknown".
     *
     * Fails with: `TransportError`.
     */
    readonly getObjectAtVersion: (options: {
        readonly objectId: string;
        readonly version: string | bigint;
    }) => Effect.Effect<VersionedObject, TransportError>;
    /**
     * What the node says about itself, including the lowest checkpoint it still
     * serves — which is how a caller tells a pruned history from a missing
     * transaction. Not a `TransportMethods` member: it reaches gRPC
     * `LedgerService.GetServiceInfo` directly (or a client's own
     * `getServiceInfo`, which is how a test double provides it).
     *
     * Fails with: `TransportError` — `status: "UNIMPLEMENTED"`, not retryable,
     * on a transport that has no such call.
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     */
    readonly getServiceInfo: () => Effect.Effect<ServiceInfo, TransportError>;
    /** Polls until a digest is visible. Fails with: `TransactionNotFound`, `TransportError`. */
    readonly waitForTransaction: <Include extends TransactionInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.WaitForTransactionOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.TransactionResult<Include>, TransactionLookupError>;
    /** Simulates a transaction. Fails with: `SimulationFailed`, `TransportError`. */
    readonly simulateTransaction: <Include extends SimulateInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.SimulateTransactionOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.SimulateTransactionResult<Include>, SimulationLookupError>;
    /** One page of transaction history. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly listTransactions: <Include extends TransactionInclude = {}>(options: SuiClientTypes.ListTransactionsOptions<Include>) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ListTransactionsResponse<Include>, TransportError>;
    /** One page of events. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly listEvents: (options: SuiClientTypes.ListEventsOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ListEventsResponse, TransportError>;
    /** The current reference gas price. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getReferenceGasPrice: (options?: SuiClientTypes.GetReferenceGasPriceOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetReferenceGasPriceResponse, TransportError>;
    /** The current system state. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getCurrentSystemState: (options?: SuiClientTypes.GetCurrentSystemStateOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetCurrentSystemStateResponse, TransportError>;
    /** The protocol config. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getProtocolConfig: (options?: SuiClientTypes.GetProtocolConfigOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetProtocolConfigResponse, TransportError>;
    /** The genesis checkpoint digest identifying the network. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getChainIdentifier: (options?: SuiClientTypes.GetChainIdentifierOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetChainIdentifierResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Move function metadata. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly getMoveFunction: (options: SuiClientTypes.GetMoveFunctionOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.GetMoveFunctionResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Verifies a zkLogin signature. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly verifyZkLoginSignature: (options: SuiClientTypes.VerifyZkLoginSignatureOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ZkLoginVerifyResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Resolves a SuiNS name to an address. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly resolveNameServiceAddress: (options: SuiClientTypes.ResolveNameServiceAddressOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.ResolveNameServiceAddressResponse, TransportError>;
    /** The default SuiNS name of an address. Fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly defaultNameServiceName: (options: SuiClientTypes.DefaultNameServiceNameOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.DefaultNameServiceNameResponse, TransportError>;
    /** Move Registry resolution. Each member fails with: `TransportError`. */
    readonly mvr: {
        readonly resolvePackage: (options: SuiClientTypes.MvrResolvePackageOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.MvrResolvePackageResponse, TransportError>;
        readonly resolveType: (options: SuiClientTypes.MvrResolveTypeOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.MvrResolveTypeResponse, TransportError>;
        readonly resolve: (options: SuiClientTypes.MvrResolveOptions) => Effect.Effect<SuiClientTypes.MvrResolveResponse, TransportError>;
    };
    /**
     * The transport's build plugin, which the transaction builder needs to
     * resolve inputs. A pure value constructor, wrapped in an Effect only so the
     * fake can refuse it. Never fails.
     */
    readonly resolveTransactionPlugin: () => Effect.Effect<TransactionPlugin>;
    /**
     * The low-level hatch: run one call against the SDK client object with the
     * Effect's `AbortSignal` already forwarded.
     *
     * Fails with the full `mapSdkError` union: `TransportError`, `ObjectNotFound`,
     * `ObjectDeleted`, `ObjectUnavailable`, `TransactionNotFound`,
     * `SimulationFailed`.
     */
    readonly use: <A>(run: (client: ClientWithCoreApi, signal: AbortSignal) => Promise<A>) => Effect.Effect<A, SuiCoreError>;
    /**
     * The retry schedule this client's reads run under, for a read made outside
     * its members — `Tx.build` retries the SDK resolver's own reads on it. It is
     * `SuiCoreReadOptions.readRetry` when the layer was given one and the
     * default otherwise (jittered exponential from 250 ms, capped at 10 s, five
     * attempts in all); in both cases it only continues while the failure is a
     * retryable `TransportError`, and every retry is logged at debug level and
     * counted in `sui_effect_core_read_retries_total` under `method`.
     *
     * Use it as `Effect.retry(effect, core.readPolicy("myRead"))`. Optional
     * only so a hand-built `Layer.mock(SuiCore, ...)` need not supply it; every
     * layer this package builds has it, and `Tx.build` falls back to the
     * default policy when it is absent.
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     */
    readonly readPolicy?: (method: string) => Schedule.Schedule<unknown, unknown>;
}
```

The mechanical tier: one member per `SuiClientTypes.TransportMethods` key,
plus the four concrete conveniences `CoreClient` adds and the `use` hatch.

### `SuiError` (type + const)

```ts
export type SuiError = TransportError | ObjectNotFound | ObjectDeleted | ObjectUnavailable | TransactionNotFound | NetworkMismatch | DecodeError | SimulationFailed | ExecutionFailed | SubmissionUnknown | NotApplied | SigningError | BuildError | PolicyDenied | JournalError | UnexpectedEffects | GraphQLUnavailable | ExtensionNotReady;
```

Every failure sui-effect can produce.

```ts
declare const SuiError: {
    readonly isRetryable: (error: unknown) => boolean;
    readonly isTaxonomy: (error: unknown) => error is SuiError;
    readonly outcome: (error: unknown, options?: {
        readonly phase?: OutcomePhase;
    }) => Outcome;
    readonly describe: (error: unknown, options?: DescribeOptions) => string;
    readonly toJson: (error: unknown) => Record<string, unknown>;
    readonly digestOf: (error: unknown) => Digest | undefined;
    readonly moveAbort: (error: unknown) => Option.Option<MoveAbortInfo>;
    readonly isMoveAbort: (error: unknown, match?: MoveAbortMatch) => error is SimulationFailed | ExecutionFailed;
}
```

The helpers every repo hand-rolls: is this worth retrying, did the
transaction land, is it even one of ours, what does an operator need to read,
what goes in a log, which transaction is it about, and which Move abort was
it. Every one takes `unknown`.

For *branching* on a failure, the taxonomy is a flat tagged union, so
`Effect.catchTags({ ObjectNotFound: ..., TransportError: ... })` in the error
channel and `Match.tagsExhaustive` over a `SuiError` value both work and are
the two documented consumer idioms; these helpers are for the questions that
are the same whatever the tag is.

### `SuiGraphQL` (class)

```ts
export declare class SuiGraphQL extends SuiGraphQL_base {
    /**
     * The tag over a client the caller built. Never fails.
     */
    static readonly layer: (client: SuiGraphQLClient) => Layer.Layer<SuiGraphQL>;
    /**
     * A client pointed at `SUI_GRAPHQL_URL`, on the network `SUI_NETWORK` names —
     * the same variable `SuiCore.layerConfig` reads, because a GraphQL endpoint
     * for one chain and a node for another is a misconfiguration no error can
     * describe after the fact.
     *
     * Fails with: `ConfigError` when either variable is missing or empty.
     */
    static readonly layerConfig: Layer.Layer<SuiGraphQL, Config.ConfigError>;
    /**
     * The tag over a client whose every call rejects with `GraphQLUnavailable`.
     *
     * This is what an application provides when it has no GraphQL endpoint and
     * still wants to build an extension that can use one. The alternative — no
     * layer at all — is a compile error in code that may never run the GraphQL
     * path; this turns the absence into the failure the extension already
     * handles, at the call it would have made.
     *
     * Never fails to build; every call through it fails.
     */
    static readonly layerUnavailable: Layer.Layer<SuiGraphQL>;
    /**
     * `layerUnavailable` with the caller's own wording, for an application that
     * knows why the endpoint is missing. Never fails to build.
     */
    static readonly layerUnavailableWith: (reason: string) => Layer.Layer<SuiGraphQL>;
    /**
     * One call against the GraphQL client, with the two failures already sorted.
     *
     * Every extension that reads GraphQL was re-deriving the same three lines:
     * yield the client, `Effect.tryPromise`, and remember that a rejection from
     * `layerUnavailable` is already a `GraphQLUnavailable` and must be passed
     * through rather than wrapped in a `TransportError` that hides why the
     * endpoint was missing. This is those three lines, once.
     *
     * `method` is what a `TransportError` is labelled with; give the query's own
     * name so a log says which read failed. `run` receives the Effect's
     * `AbortSignal` as its second argument: pass it on as the query's `signal`,
     * so `Effect.timeout` and interruption cancel the request.
     *
     * Fails with: `GraphQLUnavailable` (there is no usable endpoint),
     * `TransportError` (the call reached one and failed).
     *
     * @example
     * ```ts
     * import { SuiGraphQL } from "@unconfirmed/sui-effect"
     *
     * const chain = SuiGraphQL.query(
     *   (client, signal) => client.query({ query: "{ chainIdentifier }", variables: {}, signal }),
     *   "chainIdentifier"
     * )
     * ```
     */
    static readonly query: <A>(run: (client: SuiGraphQLClient, signal: AbortSignal) => Promise<A>, method?: string) => Effect.Effect<A, GraphQLUnavailable | TransportError, SuiGraphQL>;
}
```

The SDK's GraphQL client as a service, so an extension can require it
without constructing one.

**The service value *is* the client.** `yield* SuiGraphQL` hands back the
`SuiGraphQLClient` that was passed to `SuiGraphQL.layer(client)` — not a
wrapper with `client` on it, not an interface of methods this package
defines. `SuiGraphQL["Service"]` is therefore `SuiGraphQLClient`, which is
the type to write when a function takes the service as a parameter:

```ts
const names = (graphql: SuiGraphQL["Service"]) => graphql.query({ query, variables })
```

The requirement is spelled `SuiGraphQL` in an `Effect`'s `R`, as with every
other tag here.

### `SuiGrpcLayerOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface SuiGrpcLayerOptions extends SuiCoreReadOptions {
    readonly network: SuiClientTypes.Network;
    readonly baseUrl: string;
    /**
     * The SDK's own `timeout`, in milliseconds: a `grpc-timeout` header on each
     * call, which a server is free to ignore. To bound how long a read can
     * actually take, use `requestTimeout`.
     */
    readonly timeout?: number;
    readonly mvr?: SuiClientTypes.MvrOptions;
}
```

Options for `SuiCore.layerGrpc`: `SuiGrpcClientOptions`, plus the read
tier's `SuiCoreReadOptions`.

### `SuiLayerOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface SuiLayerOptions {
    /**
     * The chain identifier this program expects, as `getChainIdentifier` returns
     * it. When set, a layer over a node on another chain fails to build, and it
     * overrides the `KNOWN_CHAIN_IDS` entry for the client's network.
     *
     * Leave it unset for `mainnet` and `testnet`: their chain identifiers are in
     * the table and are asserted by default. `devnet`, `localnet` and any custom
     * network are regenerated, so set this when the program must pin one.
     */
    readonly chainId?: string;
    /**
     * How to retry the one `getChainIdentifier` this layer makes, when it fails
     * with a **retryable** `TransportError`.
     *
     * It matters more than one round trip usually would, because of what holds
     * the result: a `ManagedRuntime` — which is how a browser app, a Worker or a
     * Durable Object keeps one runtime per isolate — **memoizes the layer build,
     * failure included, for its whole lifetime**. One unlucky request at boot and
     * every later use of that runtime fails with the same stale
     * `TransportError`, until something disposes it. A short schedule here is the
     * cheap half of the cure; disposing the runtime when its build failed is the
     * other half (see the guide's "Application consumers" section), and
     * `layerNoDepsPinned` avoids the read altogether when the chain id is already
     * known.
     *
     * Unset, the read is made once, as it always was.
     *
     * @since 0.1.2
     */
    readonly retry?: Schedule.Schedule<unknown, TransportError>;
}
```

Options for `Sui.layerNoDepsWith`.

### `SuiObject` (interface)

```ts
export interface SuiObject<S> {
    readonly id: ObjectId;
    readonly version: Version;
    readonly digest: string;
    readonly type: ObjectType;
    readonly owner: Owner;
    readonly content: S;
    readonly ref: ObjectRef;
}
```

An object read through `Sui`, with its BCS content already decoded to `S` and
a `ref` ready to feed straight back into the transaction builder.

### `SuiSchema` (namespace)

#### `bcs` (const)

```ts
declare const bcs: <T extends Input, Input>(bcsType: BcsType<T, Input>, expectedType?: string) => Schema.Codec<T, Uint8Array>
```

Turns a BCS layout into a `Schema.Codec<T, Uint8Array>` tagged with the Move
type it belongs to.

Decoding fails with a `SchemaError` (which `Sui` maps to `DecodeError`) when
the bytes do not parse; encoding fails the same way when the value does not
serialize. The expected type is normalized with `normalizeStructTag`, so
`Coin<0x2::sui::SUI>` and its padded spelling are the same type, and a tag
with no type arguments matches every instantiation of it (see
`typeMatches`).

**`expectedType` is optional.** A Move *return value* has no struct tag —
`sui.view(recipe, bcs.Address)` reads an `address` (32 fixed bytes; `bcs.Address`
is a value, not a function) off a command result — and inventing one so the bridge has something to compare is worse than saying
there is nothing to compare. A codec built without an expected type carries
none, so nothing checks a tag before it parses: the re-serialize length check
is still what rejects mis-shaped bytes. Give the type whenever the bytes come
from an object, which is every `getObject(id, { schema })` read.

#### `decode` (const)

```ts
