```ts
//                 readonly nonce: number;
//             };
//         } | undefined;
//         readonly chain?: string | undefined;
//     };
//     readonly signedAt: Utc;
// } | {
//     readonly digest: Digest;
//     readonly _tag: "Executed";
//     readonly at: Utc;
//     readonly checkpoint?: bigint | undefined;
// } | {
//     readonly _tag: "Failed";
//     readonly digest: Digest;
//     readonly reason: {
//         readonly $kind: "MoveAbort";
//         readonly MoveAbort: {
//             readonly abortCode: bigint;
//             readonly location?: {
//                 readonly function?: number | undefined;
//                 readonly module?: string | undefined;
//                 readonly package?: string | undefined;
//                 readonly functionName?: string | undefined;
//                 readonly instruction?: number | undefined;
//             } | undefined;
//             readonly cleverError?: {
//                 readonly errorCode?: number | undefined;
//                 readonly lineNumber?: number | undefined;
//                 readonly constantName?: string | undefined;
//                 readonly constantType?: string | undefined;
//                 readonly value?: string | undefined;
//             } | undefined;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "SizeError";
//         readonly SizeError: {
//             readonly name: string;
//             readonly size: number;
//             readonly maxSize: number;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "CommandArgumentError";
//         readonly CommandArgumentError: {
//             readonly argument: number;
//             readonly name: string;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "TypeArgumentError";
//         readonly TypeArgumentError: {
//             readonly typeArgument: number;
//             readonly name: string;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "PackageUpgradeError";
//         readonly PackageUpgradeError: {
//             readonly name: string;
//             readonly digest?: string | undefined;
//             readonly packageId?: string | undefined;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "IndexError";
//         readonly IndexError: {
//             readonly index?: number | undefined;
//             readonly subresult?: number | undefined;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "CoinDenyListError";
//         readonly CoinDenyListError: {
//             readonly coinType: string;
//             readonly name: string;
//             readonly address?: string | undefined;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "CongestedObjects";
//         readonly CongestedObjects: {
//             readonly name: string;
//             readonly objects: readonly string[];
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ObjectIdError";
//         readonly ObjectIdError: {
//             readonly objectId: string;
//             readonly name?: string | undefined;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Unknown";
//         readonly message?: string | undefined;
//         readonly command?: number | undefined;
//     };
//     readonly at: Utc;
// }
```

One line of the submission journal.

`Signed` is written before the first `executeTransaction` and means the bytes
may be on the wire. `Executed`, `Failed` and `NotApplied` are terminal:
`Executed` and `Failed` because the network answered, `NotApplied` because
the transaction was proven never to have applied and never will. `Unknown`
means the answer never arrived and the bytes are kept so `Tx.reconcile` can
settle it later.

Without a terminal `NotApplied`, a durable journal would hold a proven-dead
submission as `Unknown` forever and `onUnresolved: "fail"` would refuse to
build for the life of the store.

### `JournalService` (interface)

```ts
export interface JournalService {
    /**
     * Records one entry, replacing any earlier entry for the same digest.
     *
     * Fails with: `JournalError`.
     */
    readonly put: (entry: JournalEntry) => Effect.Effect<void, JournalError>;
    /**
     * The entry for a digest, if this journal has one.
     *
     * Fails with: `JournalError`.
     */
    readonly get: (digest: Digest) => Effect.Effect<Option.Option<JournalEntry>, JournalError>;
    /**
     * Every entry still waiting for an answer (`Signed` and `Unknown`), oldest
     * first. `Tx.reconcileAll` is what a startup does with it.
     *
     * Fails with: `JournalError`.
     */
    readonly listUnresolved: Effect.Effect<ReadonlyArray<JournalEntry>, JournalError>;
}
```

Reading and writing the submission journal.

### `KeyScheme` (type)

```ts
export type KeyScheme = "ED25519" | "Secp256k1" | "Secp256r1";
```

The schemes a key at rest can be: the three a BIP-39 phrase derives on the
standard Sui paths. (`MLDSA65` has its own path family and is read only by
`Signer.fromConfig`; `ZkLogin`, `Passkey` and `MultiSig` hold no secret key.)

### `LockSenderPolicy` (type)

```ts
export type LockSenderPolicy = "whenPinned" | "always" | "never";
```

When `Tx.run` holds the sender lock.

- `"whenPinned"` (the default): for **every build**, and on through signing,
  `onSigned` and submission **only when the built bytes pin a versioned
  object or carry a gas payment** — an owned or receiving input, a gas coin,
  or a coin reservation. A coin reservation is what a recipe that uses
  `tx.gas` gets when its payer has an address balance, and it reserves the
  payer's **whole** SUI balance as of the build: a spend landing before it is
  sent makes the node refuse it. So it holds the lock, and a run that uses
  `tx.gas` first waits, before it builds, for the lock-free runs in flight on
  its payer. Bytes that pin nothing (an empty payment, gas drawn from the
  address balance at execution; pure, shared and `FundsWithdrawal` inputs)
  release it before signing, so address-balance transactions from one sender
  are sent and awaited concurrently.
- `"always"`: from build through submit on every run, the rule before 0.4.0.
- `"never"`: no lock at all.

A config provided directly rather than through `SubmitConfig.with` may still
hold the pre-0.4.0 boolean or another value: `Tx.run` reads `true` as
`"always"`, `false` as `"never"`, and anything else as `"always"`.

### `LockableSigner` (interface)

```ts
export interface LockableSigner extends Signer {
    /** Drops the secret; every later signature fails with `reason: "locked"`. Never fails. */
    readonly lock: Effect.Effect<void>;
    /** Whether `lock` has run. Never fails. */
    readonly locked: Effect.Effect<boolean>;
}
```

A `Signer` that holds a secret and can drop it.

`lock` is **disposal, one way**: it zeroes the secret bytes the signer owns,
zeroes the keypair's own copy where the SDK keeps one, and releases the
keypair. Afterwards both members fail with `SigningError { reason:
"locked" }`; there is no unlock — open the account again (`Keystore.unlock`,
`Signer.fromMnemonic`) for a new signer. `lock` is idempotent and never
fails.

A `LockableSigner` **is** a `Signer`: every function that takes a signer
takes this one, and it carries no secret either — the keypair lives in a
closure, not on the value.

### `MnemonicLength` (type)

```ts
export type MnemonicLength = 12 | 15 | 18 | 21 | 24;
```

The BIP-39 word counts: 128 to 256 bits of entropy in 32-bit steps.

### `OutflowClaim` (const)

```ts
declare const OutflowClaim: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly coinType: CoinType; readonly max: bigint; }
```

The most of one coin the sender may lose, in base units, gas excluded.

### `Parties` (interface)

```ts
export interface Parties {
    readonly sender: SuiAddress;
    readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress | undefined;
}
```

Whose transaction it is. Gas spent by whoever pays it is never an outflow.

A `Built` is a `Parties` as it is: `sender`, and `gasOwner` only when someone
else pays.

### `PasskeyProvider` (interface)

```ts
export interface PasskeyProvider {
    readonly create: () => Promise<{
        readonly rawId: ArrayBuffer;
        readonly response: {
            readonly getPublicKey: () => ArrayBuffer | null;
        };
    }>;
    readonly get: (challenge: Uint8Array, credentialId?: Uint8Array) => Promise<{
        readonly response: {
            readonly authenticatorData: ArrayBuffer;
            readonly clientDataJSON: ArrayBuffer;
            readonly signature: ArrayBuffer;
        };
    }>;
}
```

What a passkey signer asks the authenticator for, structurally: the SDK's
`PasskeyProvider` from `@mysten/sui/keypairs/passkey` (its
`BrowserPasskeyProvider` is one) without the DOM types it is declared with,
so a React Native or extension provider can be typed without a DOM `lib`.
`create()` answers the `navigator.credentials.create()` credential (its
`rawId` and `response.getPublicKey()`), `get()` the
`navigator.credentials.get()` assertion for `challenge`.

### `Prepared` (interface)

```ts
export interface Prepared {
    readonly built: Built;
    readonly simulation: Simulation;
    readonly verified?: Verified;
}
```

Built bytes and what the lifecycle learned about them before anything is
signed: the simulation of exactly those bytes, and the verified intent when
one was declared. What `prepare` answers and what `Tx.run`'s
`approve` is shown.

`built.gas` is the gas the bytes carry (budget, price, payer, payment and
where it comes from) and `built.gasOwner` the sponsor, when there is one;
`simulation` has `Executed`'s accessors (`created`, `transferred`,
`balanceChange`, ...), and `simulation.gasUsedTotal` is the gas it is
expected to cost. `verified` is present exactly when an intent was given,
and is the `Built` to sign when it is.

### `ReconcileInput` (type)

```ts
export type ReconcileInput = Digest | Signed | SubmissionUnknown;
```

What `Tx.reconcile` can be asked about.

### `Reconciled` (const)

```ts
declare const Reconciled: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly _tag: "ExecutionFailed";
//     readonly error: ExecutionFailed;
// } | {
//     readonly _tag: "NotApplied";
//     readonly error: NotApplied;
// } | {
//     readonly _tag: "SubmissionUnknown";
//     readonly error: SubmissionUnknown;
// } | {
//     readonly _tag: "Executed";
//     readonly executed: Executed;
// }
```

What one entry of `Tx.reconcileAll` settled to, as a tagged union.

Every case carries **one** discriminator in the same place: `_tag` is
`"Executed"`, `"ExecutionFailed"`, `"NotApplied"` or `"SubmissionUnknown"`,
and the payload is `executed` for the first and `error` for the other three.
Before 0.1.2 this was a bare union of an `Executed` (which has no `_tag`) and
three errors (which do), so the only way to tell a success from a failure was
`"_tag" in entry` — a shape nothing could `Schema.match` or serialize.

### `RemoteSigner` (interface)

```ts
export interface RemoteSigner {
    readonly address: SuiAddress;
    readonly scheme: SignatureScheme;
    readonly signTransaction: (bytes: Uint8Array) => Effect.Effect<string, SigningError>;
    readonly signPersonalMessage?: (bytes: Uint8Array) => Effect.Effect<string, SigningError>;
}
```

What `remote` needs to know about a credential it does not hold.

`address` is not optional and is not derived: a remote signer **must report
the address it signs as**. `Tx.sign` and `Tx.cosign` compare it with the
transaction's sender and gas owner and refuse a mismatch, which is the only
thing standing between a misconfigured KMS key and an `INVALID_ARGUMENT`
rejection that `Tx.submit` can only report as `SubmissionUnknown`.

### `RunError` (type)

```ts
export type RunError = BuildError | SimulationFailed | PolicyDenied | SigningError | SubmitError | TransportError;
```

Everything `run` can fail with, as one name: `SubmitError` plus
what building, preflighting and signing can produce.

### `ScreenRequest` (interface)

```ts
export interface ScreenRequest {
    readonly intent: Intent;
    /** `describeEffects` of the simulation. Never empty. */
    readonly effects: ReadonlyArray<string>;
    /** `describeClaims` of the intent. Empty when it claims nothing. */
    readonly claims: ReadonlyArray<string>;
}
```

What a screen is shown: the sentence, and both things it is compared with.

### `ScreenedLine` (const)

```ts
declare const ScreenedLine: Schema
// decodes to: { readonly text: string; readonly inScope: number; }
```

One rendered line and the probability that the declared summary covers it.

### `Screening` (const)

```ts
declare const Screening: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly effects: readonly {
//         readonly text: string;
//         readonly inScope: number;
//     }[];
//     readonly claims: readonly {
//         readonly text: string;
//         readonly inScope: number;
//     }[];
//     readonly specificity: number;
//     readonly injection: number;
// }
```

What a semantic screen answered: probabilities, never a decision. The
decision is `Gate`'s thresholds, applied in code by `Tx.verify`.

### `SecretSealer` (interface)

```ts
export interface SecretSealer {
    /** Fails with: `SigningError`. */
    readonly seal: (secret: Redacted.Redacted<Uint8Array>, aad: Uint8Array) => Effect.Effect<string, SigningError>;
    /** Fails with: `SigningError` (`reason: "locked"` when the keystore is locked is the convention). */
    readonly unseal: (sealed: string, aad: Uint8Array) => Effect.Effect<Redacted.Redacted<Uint8Array>, SigningError>;
}
```

Seals and unseals a secret for storage. **sui-effect ships no sealer**: the
keystore that owns the wrapping key (WebCrypto AES-GCM under a key the
platform keeps, a passphrase-derived key, the OS keychain) implements this.

`aad` is additional authenticated data the sealer must bind the blob to —
here, the UTF-8 bytes of the session's address — so a sealed key copied
under another account's entry does not unseal. A sealer that returns its
input unchanged stores the ephemeral key in plain text; that is a decision
the caller makes by writing one, never a default.

### `Signature` (const)

Re-exported from `@unconfirmed/sui-effect`.

### `SignatureScheme` (type)

```ts
type SignatureScheme = 'ED25519' | 'Secp256k1' | 'Secp256r1' | 'MultiSig' | 'ZkLogin' | 'Passkey' | 'MLDSA65';
```

### `Signed` (const)

```ts
declare const Signed: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     readonly digest: Digest;
//     readonly sender: SuiAddress;
//     readonly signatures: readonly Signature[];
//     readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress | undefined;
//     readonly expiration?: {
//         readonly $kind: "None";
//         readonly None: true;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Epoch";
//         readonly Epoch: bigint;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ValidDuring";
//         readonly ValidDuring: {
//             readonly minEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly minTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly chain: string;
//             readonly nonce: number;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Validity";
//         readonly Validity: {
//             readonly allowedProposers: {
//                 readonly epoch: bigint;
//                 readonly proposers: readonly number[];
//             } | null;
//             readonly minEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly minTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly chain: string;
//             readonly nonce: number;
//         };
//     } | undefined;
//     readonly chain?: string | undefined;
// }
```

The schema of `Signed`.

### `Signer` (interface + const)

```ts
export interface Signer {
    /** The Sui address this signer signs as. */
    readonly address: SuiAddress;
    /** The signature scheme of the credential. */
    readonly scheme: SignatureScheme;
    /**
     * Signs transaction bytes with the `TransactionData` intent.
     *
     * Fails with: `SigningError`.
     */
    readonly signTransaction: (bytes: Uint8Array) => Effect.Effect<Signature, SigningError>;
    /**
     * Signs a personal message with the `PersonalMessage` intent.
     *
     * Fails with: `SigningError`.
     */
    readonly signPersonalMessage: (bytes: Uint8Array) => Effect.Effect<Signature, SigningError>;
    /**
     * The last epoch this credential can sign for, when it is session-bound (a
     * zkLogin proof's `maxEpoch`). Absent for a credential that does not expire.
     * `Tx.run` keeps the built bytes' validity window inside it, and a signer
     * whose bound has passed fails with `SigningError { reason: "expired" }`.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    readonly maxEpoch?: bigint;
}
```

A credential: who it signs as, how, and the two things it can sign.

Both members fail with `SigningError` and nothing else: a signer that has to
reach a KMS or a wallet wraps its own transport failure in the `cause`, so a
caller's error union does not grow a branch per credential kind.

```ts
declare const Signer: {
    readonly fromSdkSigner: (keypair: SdkSigner) => Signer;
    readonly fromKeypair: (keypair: Keypair) => Signer;
    readonly fromConfig: (name?: string) => Effect.Effect<Signer, Config.ConfigError>;
    readonly ephemeral: Effect.Effect<Signer, never, never>;
    readonly remote: (signer: RemoteSigner) => Signer;
    readonly fromMnemonic: (phrase: Redacted.Redacted<string> | string, options?: FromMnemonicOptions) => Effect.Effect<LockableSigner, KeyError>;
    readonly fromSecretKey: (secret: Redacted.Redacted<string> | string | Uint8Array, options?: FromSecretKeyOptions) => Effect.Effect<LockableSigner, KeyError>;
    readonly generateMnemonic: (words?: MnemonicLength) => Effect.Effect<Redacted.Redacted<string>, KeyError>;
    readonly derivationPath: (scheme: KeyScheme, account?: number) => string;
    readonly scoped: <E, R>(acquire: Effect.Effect<LockableSigner, E, R>) => Effect.Effect<LockableSigner, E, R | Scope.Scope>;
    readonly zkLogin: (options: import("./ZkLoginSigner.ts").ZkLoginSignerOptions) => Effect.Effect<Signer, SigningError, never>;
    readonly passkey: (options: {
        readonly publicKey: Uint8Array;
        readonly provider: import("./AccountSigners.ts").PasskeyProvider;
        readonly credentialId?: Uint8Array;
    }) => Effect.Effect<Signer, SigningError, never>;
    readonly createPasskey: (provider: import("./AccountSigners.ts").PasskeyProvider) => Effect.Effect<{
        readonly signer: Signer;
        readonly publicKey: Uint8Array;
        readonly credentialId?: Uint8Array;
    }, SigningError, never>;
    readonly wallet: (options: {
        readonly wallet: import("./AccountSigners.ts").StandardWallet;
        readonly account: import("./AccountSigners.ts").StandardWalletAccount;
        readonly chain: string;
        readonly scheme?: SignatureScheme;
    }) => Effect.Effect<Signer, SigningError, never>;
}
```

The constructors, namespaced the way the spec spells them:
`Signer.fromSdkSigner`, `Signer.fromKeypair`, `Signer.fromConfig`,
`Signer.ephemeral`, `Signer.remote`; for keys the process holds
`Signer.fromMnemonic`, `Signer.fromSecretKey`, `Signer.generateMnemonic`,
`Signer.derivationPath` and `Signer.scoped`; and the account signers
(0.5.0) `Signer.zkLogin`, `Signer.passkey`, `Signer.createPasskey`,
`Signer.wallet`. The type `Signer` is the interface above.

### `StandardWallet` (interface)

```ts
export interface StandardWallet {
    readonly name?: string;
    readonly features: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;
}
```

A Wallet Standard wallet, structurally: anything with a `features` record.
A `Wallet` from `@wallet-standard/base` (or a `WalletWithRequiredFeatures`
from `@mysten/wallet-standard`) is one.

### `StandardWalletAccount` (interface)

```ts
export interface StandardWalletAccount {
    readonly address: string;
    readonly publicKey: ArrayLike<number>;
    readonly chains: ReadonlyArray<string>;
    readonly features?: ReadonlyArray<string>;
}
```

A Wallet Standard account, structurally: what `@wallet-standard/base`'s
`WalletAccount` carries and this module reads.

### `SubmitConfig` (const)

```ts
declare const SubmitConfig: Context.Reference<SubmitConfigService> & {
    /** The settings in force when nothing overrides them. */
    defaults: SubmitConfigService;
    /**
     * A `Layer` that puts `defaults` in force with `overrides` applied, for an
     * application or a test that sets its lifecycle policy once at the edge.
     * Never fails, requires nothing.
     */
    layer: (overrides: SubmitConfigOverrides) => Layer.Layer<never>;
    /**
     * The config in force with `overrides` applied, provided to one effect:
     * the `.pipe`-able form of `layer`, merging over what is in force rather
     * than over `defaults`. Never fails, requires nothing.
     */
    with: (overrides: SubmitConfigOverrides) => <A, E, R>(effect: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>) => Effect.Effect<A, E, R>;
}
```

The lifecycle settings.

Because this is a `Context.Reference` and not a service, it never appears in
an `R`: a one-shot script gets the defaults with no wiring, and an
application overrides what it cares about with
`SubmitConfig.with` (one effect), which merges over the config **in
force** so an outer override survives an inner one, or
`SubmitConfig.layer` (a whole application), which spreads
`SubmitConfig.defaults` for you.

### `SubmitConfigOverrides` (type)

```ts
export type SubmitConfigOverrides = {
    readonly [K in keyof SubmitConfigService]?: K extends DurationField ? Duration.Input : K extends "lockSender" ? LockSenderPolicy | boolean : SubmitConfigService[K];
};
```

What `SubmitConfig.with` and `SubmitConfig.layer` accept: any
subset of `SubmitConfigService`, with every duration given as a
`Duration.Input` — `"3 seconds"`, `Duration.seconds(3)`, a number of
milliseconds — and converted on the way in. The value in force (what
`yield* SubmitConfig` answers) always holds `Duration`s. `lockSender` also
takes the boolean it was before 0.4.0: `true` is `"always"`, `false`
`"never"`.

### `SubmitConfigService` (interface)

```ts
export interface SubmitConfigService {
    /**
     * The expiration `Tx.build` sets when the recipe set none.
     *
     * `"validDuring"` bounds the transaction to the current epoch and the next,
     * and names the chain: it is the only one that both satisfies the validator
     * rule (a transaction must have address-owned inputs *or* an expiration of at
     * most two epochs) and stops bytes signed for one chain from landing on
     * another. It costs one `getCurrentSystemState` read per build.
     * `"epoch"` costs the same read and carries no chain guard. `"none"` leaves
     * the transaction valid forever, which makes
     * `NotApplied { evidence: "expired" }` unreachable.
     */
    readonly expiration: ExpirationPolicy;
    /**
     * An **additional** wall-clock bound on a `"validDuring"` expiration, as
     * `maxTimestamp`.
     *
     * Unset by default, because no Sui network accepts one yet: a devnet node on
     * protocol 100 refuses any transaction carrying a timestamp bound with
     * `Feature is not supported: Timestamp-based transaction expiration is not
     * yet supported`, whether or not epochs are set alongside it. Setting this
     * produces bytes the current protocol rejects at build time; it is here so
     * that a network which does support them needs no new API, and so that
     * `Tx.reconcile`'s wall-clock expiry rule has something to read.
     */
    readonly validFor?: Duration.Duration;
    /**
     * The largest gas budget `Tx.build` will accept. The budget itself is chosen
     * by the node's simulation during build; this is the ceiling above which
     * building fails with `PolicyDenied { rule: "maxGasBudget" }` rather than
     * signing an expensive mistake.
     */
    readonly maxGasBudget: Mist;
    /**
     * An extra simulate before signing, and the place spend limits and target
     * policies plug in. It fails with `PolicyDenied` and nothing else, so
     * `Tx.run` stays typed.
     */
    readonly preflight?: (simulation: Simulation) => Effect.Effect<void, PolicyDenied>;
    /**
     * When `Tx.run` holds the sender lock; see `LockSenderPolicy`.
     * Defaults to `"whenPinned"`. Before 0.4.0 this was a boolean, and `true` /
     * `false` given as overrides still mean `"always"` / `"never"`.
     */
    readonly lockSender: LockSenderPolicy;
    /**
     * How an unset gas payment is chosen; see `GasPaymentPolicy`.
     * Defaults to `"preferAddressBalance"`.
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     */
    readonly gasPayment: GasPaymentPolicy;
    /**
     * How `Tx.submit` re-sends the identical bytes after a retryable transport
     * failure or a timeout. It never rebuilds, so a retry can only ever land the
     * transaction the caller already signed.
     */
    readonly resubmit: Schedule.Schedule<unknown>;
    /** How many times `Tx.submit` sends the bytes in total, the first try included. */
    readonly resubmitAttempts: number;
    /**
     * How long one `executeTransaction` may take before `Tx.submit` treats it as
     * a retryable transport failure. A timeout is the case `Tx.submit` exists
     * for: the bytes may well have reached the network.
     */
    readonly executeTimeout: Duration.Duration;
    /**
     * How far `chainTime` must pass a transaction's recorded `maxTimestamp`
     * before `Tx.reconcile` is willing to call it `NotApplied`. It covers the
     * skew between the node's clock, the Clock object and the validators.
     *
     * It applies to the wall-clock rule only. The epoch rule needs no margin:
     * an epoch is a consensus fact, not a reading of a clock, so once the
     * current epoch is past a transaction's `maxEpoch` it is simply over.
     */
    readonly expiryMargin: Duration.Duration;
    /**
     * Whether a closed expiration window may be evidence at all, and under which
     * rule. See `ExpiryEvidencePolicy`. Defaults to `"epochThenMiss"`.
     */
    readonly expiryEvidence: ExpiryEvidencePolicy;
    /**
     * How long `Tx.reconcile` waits between the first observation of a closed
     * expiration window and the second one it needs before it will answer
     * `NotApplied { evidence: "expired" }`.
     *
     * It is a `Clock` sleep, so a test drives it with `TestClock` and a test that
     * does not care sets it to zero.
     */
    readonly reconcileRecheck: Duration.Duration;
    /**
     * Whether `Tx.submit` waits for a successful execution to become visible to
     * reads before it returns and releases the sender lock. Defaults to `true`.
     */
    readonly awaitVisibility: boolean;
    /**
     * How long that wait may take. A wait that times out or fails **never**
     * changes the outcome: the transaction executed, and `Tx.submit` returns the
     * `Executed` it already has after logging the failure.
     */
    readonly visibilityTimeout: Duration.Duration;
    /**
     * The nonce `Tx.build` stamps on a default `ValidDuring` expiration, which is
     * what makes two otherwise identical transactions from one address in one
     * epoch different bytes with different digests.
     *
     * The default draws a `u32` from Effect's `Random`, which is process-local
     * and does not survive a restart: independent 32-bit draws collide with
     * probability about 1.16% after ten thousand such transactions. **A collision
     * is not a second execution.** Identical bytes have one digest and one
     * journal key, so the second build is the same transaction as the first; the
     * failure mode is a transaction that cannot be sent again because the first
     * one already occupied its digest, not a duplicated intent.
     *
     * Supply a monotonic allocator when that matters — a counter in the same
     * store the journal uses, an id service, anything that survives a restart:
     * `nonce: Effect.map(counter.next, (n) => n % 4_294_967_296)`. The value must
     * be an integer in `[0, 2^32)`; anything else fails the build with
     * `BuildError`. A journal-backed allocator is deferred (DESIGN §15).
     */
    readonly nonce: Effect.Effect<number>;
}
```

Every decision `Tx.build`, `Tx.submit` and `Tx.reconcile` read from context.

### `SubmitError` (type)

```ts
export type SubmitError = ExecutionFailed | NotApplied | SubmissionUnknown | JournalError | TransportError;
```

Everything `submit` can fail with, as one name.

An extension that wraps a submission spells its own errors plus this, rather
than repeating four tags that will grow with the taxonomy.

### `SubmitViaError` (type)

```ts
export type SubmitViaError = ExecutionFailed | NotApplied | SubmissionUnknown | JournalError;
```

Everything `submitVia` can fail with, before the sender's own errors.

### `SubmitViaReply` (type)

```ts
export type SubmitViaReply = unknown;
```

What a third party answers when it has submitted your bytes.

Three shapes are understood, in this order: an SDK `TransactionResult` (the
service ran `executeTransaction` and passed the whole thing on), a reduced
execute envelope (anything with a `digest` or an `effects`, decoded through
`Executed.fromPartial`), and a bare digest string. Anything else — a
`void`, an acknowledgement with no digest — means "ask the chain", and
`submitVia` reconciles by the digest it already has, which is the digest of
the bytes it handed over.

### `TransactionExpiration` (const)

Re-exported from `@unconfirmed/sui-effect`.

### `Tx` (const)

```ts
declare const Tx: {
    readonly build: (input: Transaction | Recipe, opts: {
        readonly sender: SuiAddress;
        readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress;
        /**
         * The last epoch the transaction may be valid in: a signer's session
         * bound (`Signer.maxEpoch`). The default expiration's `maxEpoch` is capped
         * at it, and a bound already below the current epoch fails the build
         * before anything resolves.
         *
         * @since 0.5.0
         */
        readonly maxEpoch?: bigint;
    }) => Effect.Effect<Built, TransportError | SimulationFailed | BuildError | PolicyDenied, Sui>;
    readonly sign: (built: Built, signer: Signer) => Effect.Effect<Signed, SigningError, never>;
    readonly cosign: (signed: Signed, signer: Signer) => Effect.Effect<Signed, SigningError, never>;
    readonly fromBytes: (bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>) => Effect.Effect<Built, BuildError, Sui>;
    readonly attach: (target: Built | Signed, signature: string) => Effect.Effect<Signed, TransportError | SigningError, Sui>;
    readonly sponsored: (opts: {
        readonly sender: SuiAddress;
        readonly gasOwner: SuiAddress;
    }) => (recipe: Recipe) => Recipe;
    readonly gaslessStablecoinTransfer: (opts: {
        readonly coinType: CoinType;
        readonly amount: bigint;
        readonly recipient: SuiAddress;
        /**
         * Fail the build unless the resolved gas price and budget are both zero.
         *
         * @since 0.4.0
         */
        readonly requireZeroGas?: boolean;
    }) => Recipe;
    readonly sendFunds: (opts: {
        readonly coinType?: CoinType;
        readonly amount: bigint;
        readonly recipient: SuiAddress;
        readonly useGasCoin?: boolean;
    }) => Recipe;
    readonly planFold: (opts: {
        readonly owner: SuiAddress;
        readonly coinType?: CoinType;
        readonly maxCoinsPerTransaction?: number;
    }) => Effect.Effect<FoldPlan, TransportError, Sui>;
    readonly fold: (batch: FoldBatch) => Recipe;
    readonly foldCoins: <E = never>(opts: {
        readonly signer: Signer;
        /** One coin type to fold; every coin type the signer holds coins of when omitted. */
        readonly coinType?: CoinType;
        /** At most this many coins per transaction: 1 to 500, 500 by default. */
        readonly maxCoinsPerTransaction?: number;
        /**
         * A plan from `Tx.planFold`, run as given rather than re-planned.
         *
         * @since 0.5.0
         */
        readonly plan?: FoldPlan;
        /**
         * `Tx.run`'s `approve`, per batch, with the batch it is about to fold.
         *
         * @since 0.5.0
         */
        readonly approve?: (prepared: Prepared, batch: FoldBatch) => Effect.Effect<void, E>;
    }) => Effect.Effect<FoldResult, RunError | E, Sui>;
    readonly foldMovement: (batch: FoldBatch, effects: Simulation | Executed) => import("./Fold.ts").FoldMovement;
    readonly submit: (signed: Signed) => Effect.Effect<Executed, SubmitError, Sui>;
    readonly submitVia: <E, R>(signed: Signed, send: (bytes: Uint8Array, signatures: ReadonlyArray<Signature>) => Effect.Effect<SubmitViaReply, E, R>) => Effect.Effect<Executed, SubmitViaError | E, Sui | R>;
    readonly verify: (built: Built, intent: Intent) => Effect.Effect<Verified, TransportError | SimulationFailed | BuildError | PolicyDenied, Sui>;
    readonly prepare: (input: Built | Transaction | Recipe, opts: {
        readonly sender: SuiAddress;
        readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress;
        /** What the transaction is declared to do; `verified` is present when given. */
        readonly intent?: Intent;
        /** A session bound for what is built here (`Signer.maxEpoch`); see `Tx.build`. Ignored for a `Built`. */
        readonly maxEpoch?: bigint;
    }) => Effect.Effect<Prepared, TransportError | SimulationFailed | BuildError | PolicyDenied, Sui>;
    readonly reconcile: (input: ReconcileInput) => Effect.Effect<Executed, ExecutionFailed | SubmissionUnknown | NotApplied, Sui>;
    readonly resubmit: (input: SubmissionUnknown | Signed) => Effect.Effect<Executed, SubmitError, Sui>;
    readonly recorded: (digest: Digest) => Effect.Effect<Option.Option<JournalEntry>, JournalError, never>;
    readonly run: <E = never>(recipe: Transaction | Recipe, opts: {
        readonly signer: Signer;
        readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress;
        /**
         * The gas owner's signer, for a sponsored transaction. Required whenever
         * the bytes name a gas owner that is not the sender.
         */
        readonly sponsor?: Signer;
        /**
         * Called with the signed bytes **after every signature is on them and
         * before the first `executeTransaction`**, which is the one moment a
         * consumer's own record has to be written: the digest is final from here
         * on, and anything that happens next may have reached the network.
         *
         * `Tx.submit` already writes its `Signed` journal entry at this point; this
         * is for the record the journal does not hold — a domain row joining the
         * digest to a batch, an outbox, a log line an operator greps. It runs
         * inside the sender lock, so it is ordered with the submission it belongs
         * to.
         *
         * Failing it fails the run **before anything is sent**, which is why its
         * error is a `JournalError`: that is the taxonomy's "the record could not
         * be written and nothing has gone out yet", it is already in `Tx.run`'s
         * union, and it is `not_applied`, so the documented retry idiom is correct.
         * Map your own persistence failure into it
         * (`Effect.mapError((cause) => new JournalError({ cause }))`).
         *
         * @since 0.1.2
         */
        readonly onSigned?: (signed: Signed) => Effect.Effect<void, JournalError>;
        /**
         * What this transaction is declared to do. When present, the built bytes
         * go through `verify` before anything is signed, sharing its
         * simulate with `SubmitConfig.preflight`. `Gate.requireIntent` makes it
         * mandatory.
         *
         * @since 0.5.0
         */
        readonly intent?: Intent;
        /**
         * Shown the `Prepared` transaction — the built bytes with their gas,
         * the simulation of exactly those bytes, the verified intent — **after**
         * `preflight` and the intent checks and **before the first signature**.
         * Succeeding approves; failing refuses with the caller's own error (a
         * wallet's `UserRejected`), which `Tx.run` fails with, nothing signed and
         * nothing journalled. It runs where signing runs: under the sender lock
         * when the bytes pin something, after it is released when they do not,
         * so a person taking their time never holds an address-balance account's
         * lock. Giving it always costs the simulate it is shown (the build's own,
         * when the build made one of exactly these bytes).
         *
         * @since 0.5.0
         */
        readonly approve?: (prepared: Prepared) => Effect.Effect<void, E>;
    }) => Effect.Effect<Executed, TransportError | SimulationFailed | ExecutionFailed | SubmissionUnknown | NotApplied | SigningError | BuildError | PolicyDenied | JournalError | E, Sui>;
    readonly reconcileAll: (opts?: {
        /**
         * Re-send, as identical bytes, every entry that is still
         * `SubmissionUnknown` after reconciling. Defaults to `false`.
         *
         * @since 0.4.0
         */
        readonly resubmit?: boolean;
    } | undefined) => Effect.Effect<readonly ({
        readonly _tag: "ExecutionFailed";
        readonly error: ExecutionFailed;
    } | {
        readonly _tag: "SubmissionUnknown";
        readonly error: SubmissionUnknown;
    } | {
        readonly _tag: "NotApplied";
        readonly error: NotApplied;
    } | {
        readonly _tag: "Executed";
        readonly executed: Executed;
    })[], JournalError, Sui>;
}
```

The lifecycle, namespaced the way the spec spells it: `Tx.build`, `Tx.sign`,
`Tx.cosign`, `Tx.fromBytes`, `Tx.attach`, `Tx.sponsored`,
`Tx.gaslessStablecoinTransfer`, `Tx.sendFunds`, `Tx.planFold`, `Tx.fold`,
`Tx.foldCoins`, `Tx.foldMovement`, `Tx.submit`, `Tx.submitVia`, `Tx.verify`,
`Tx.prepare`, `Tx.reconcile`, `Tx.resubmit`, `Tx.recorded`, `Tx.run`,
`Tx.reconcileAll`.

### `UNRESOLVED_TAGS` (const)

```ts
declare const UNRESOLVED_TAGS: readonly ["Signed", "Unknown"]
```

The tags whose entries still need an answer from the network.

### `Verdict` (const)

```ts
declare const Verdict: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly _tag: "Screened";
//     readonly screening: {
//         readonly effects: readonly {
//             readonly text: string;
//             readonly inScope: number;
//         }[];
//         readonly claims: readonly {
//             readonly text: string;
//             readonly inScope: number;
//         }[];
//         readonly specificity: number;
//         readonly injection: number;
//     };
// } | {
//     readonly _tag: "Unscreened";
//     readonly reason: string;
// }
```

How far a transaction was checked. The claims always held, or there would be
a `PolicyDenied` instead of a verdict; what varies is whether anything read
the sentence. `Unscreened` is never silent: it is on the record with a reason.

### `Verified` (const)

```ts
declare const Verified: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
//     readonly digest: Digest;
//     readonly sender: SuiAddress;
//     readonly gas: {
//         readonly budget: Mist;
//         readonly price: Mist;
//         readonly owner: SuiAddress;
//         readonly payment: readonly {
//             readonly objectId: ObjectId;
//             readonly version: Version;
//             readonly digest: string;
//         }[];
//         readonly source: "addressBalance" | "coinReservation" | "coins";
//     };
//     readonly intent: {
//         readonly summary: string;
//         readonly author: "user" | "task" | "dapp";
//         readonly claims: {
//             readonly outflows: readonly {
//                 readonly coinType: CoinType;
//                 readonly max: bigint;
//             }[];
//             readonly recipients: readonly SuiAddress[];
//             readonly objects: readonly ObjectId[];
//         };
//     };
//     readonly verdict: {
//         readonly _tag: "Screened";
//         readonly screening: {
//             readonly effects: readonly {
//                 readonly text: string;
//                 readonly inScope: number;
//             }[];
//             readonly claims: readonly {
//                 readonly text: string;
//                 readonly inScope: number;
//             }[];
//             readonly specificity: number;
//             readonly injection: number;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly _tag: "Unscreened";
//         readonly reason: string;
//     };
//     readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress | undefined;
//     readonly expiration?: {
//         readonly $kind: "None";
//         readonly None: true;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Epoch";
//         readonly Epoch: bigint;
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "ValidDuring";
//         readonly ValidDuring: {
//             readonly minEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly minTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly chain: string;
//             readonly nonce: number;
//         };
//     } | {
//         readonly $kind: "Validity";
//         readonly Validity: {
//             readonly allowedProposers: {
//                 readonly epoch: bigint;
//                 readonly proposers: readonly number[];
//             } | null;
//             readonly minEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxEpoch: bigint | null;
//             readonly minTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly maxTimestamp: bigint | null;
//             readonly chain: string;
//             readonly nonce: number;
//         };
//     } | undefined;
//     readonly chain?: string | undefined;
//     readonly simulation?: SimulationClass | undefined;
// } & Brand<"Verified">
```

A `Built` whose simulated effects stayed inside a declared
`Intent`. Structurally a `Built`, so `Tx.sign` takes it as it is; the
brand is what says `Tx.verify` produced it.

### `Verify` (const)

```ts
declare const Verify: {
    readonly verifyTransactionSignature: (bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>, signature: string, options?: VerifyOptions | undefined) => Effect.Effect<PublicKey, TransportError | SigningError, Sui>;
    readonly verifyPersonalMessageSignature: (message: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>, signature: string, options?: VerifyOptions | undefined) => Effect.Effect<PublicKey, TransportError | SigningError, Sui>;
}
```

Signature verification, namespaced the way the SDK spells it:
`Verify.verifyTransactionSignature`, `Verify.verifyPersonalMessageSignature`.
Both have `R = Sui`.

### `VerifyOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface VerifyOptions {
    /**
     * The address the signature must have been produced by. Compared with the
     * address the SDK derives from the signature's public key, which is always
     * the padded, normalized form — so it is typed `SuiAddress`; normalize a
     * shorthand with `SuiAddress.normalize` first.
     */
    readonly address?: SuiAddress;
}
```

The options both verifiers take.

### `ZkLogin` (const)

```ts
declare const ZkLogin: {
    readonly Session: Schema.Struct<{
        readonly address: Schema.brand<Schema.decodeTo<Schema.toType<Schema.String>, Schema.String, never, never>, "SuiAddress"> & {
            normalize: (input: string) => SuiAddress;
            Strict: Schema.brand<Schema.decodeTo<Schema.toType<Schema.String>, Schema.String, never, never>, "SuiAddress">;
        };
        readonly maxEpoch: Schema.BigIntFromString;
        readonly proof: Schema.Struct<{
            readonly proofPoints: Schema.Struct<{
                readonly a: Schema.$Array<Schema.String>;
                readonly b: Schema.$Array<Schema.$Array<Schema.String>>;
                readonly c: Schema.$Array<Schema.String>;
            }>;
            readonly issBase64Details: Schema.Struct<{
                readonly value: Schema.String;
                readonly indexMod4: Schema.Int;
            }>;
            readonly headerBase64: Schema.String;
            readonly addressSeed: Schema.String;
        }>;
        readonly expiresAt: Schema.optionalKey<Schema.Finite>;
    }>;
    readonly SignatureInputs: Schema.Struct<{
        readonly proofPoints: Schema.Struct<{
            readonly a: Schema.$Array<Schema.String>;
            readonly b: Schema.$Array<Schema.$Array<Schema.String>>;
            readonly c: Schema.$Array<Schema.String>;
        }>;
        readonly issBase64Details: Schema.Struct<{
            readonly value: Schema.String;
            readonly indexMod4: Schema.Int;
        }>;
        readonly headerBase64: Schema.String;
        readonly addressSeed: Schema.String;
    }>;
    readonly StoredSession: Schema.Struct<{
        readonly session: Schema.Struct<{
            readonly address: Schema.brand<Schema.decodeTo<Schema.toType<Schema.String>, Schema.String, never, never>, "SuiAddress"> & {
                normalize: (input: string) => SuiAddress;
                Strict: Schema.brand<Schema.decodeTo<Schema.toType<Schema.String>, Schema.String, never, never>, "SuiAddress">;
            };
            readonly maxEpoch: Schema.BigIntFromString;
            readonly proof: Schema.Struct<{
                readonly proofPoints: Schema.Struct<{
                    readonly a: Schema.$Array<Schema.String>;
                    readonly b: Schema.$Array<Schema.$Array<Schema.String>>;
                    readonly c: Schema.$Array<Schema.String>;
                }>;
                readonly issBase64Details: Schema.Struct<{
                    readonly value: Schema.String;
                    readonly indexMod4: Schema.Int;
                }>;
                readonly headerBase64: Schema.String;
                readonly addressSeed: Schema.String;
            }>;
            readonly expiresAt: Schema.optionalKey<Schema.Finite>;
        }>;
        readonly ephemeral: Schema.Struct<{
            readonly scheme: Schema.Literal<"ED25519">;
            readonly sealed: Schema.String;
        }>;
    }>;
    readonly save: (store: KeyValueStore.KeyValueStore, key: string, input: {
        readonly session: ZkLoginSession;
        readonly ephemeralSecretKey: Redacted.Redacted<Uint8Array>;
        readonly sealer: SecretSealer;
    }) => Effect.Effect<void, SigningError, never>;
    readonly load: (store: KeyValueStore.KeyValueStore, key: string, options: {
        readonly sealer: SecretSealer;
    }) => Effect.Effect<Option.Option<Signer>, SigningError, never>;
    readonly remove: (store: KeyValueStore.KeyValueStore, key: string) => Effect.Effect<void, SigningError, never>;
}
```

The zkLogin session schemas and store, namespaced: `ZkLogin.Session`,
`ZkLogin.SignatureInputs`, `ZkLogin.StoredSession`, `ZkLogin.save`,
`ZkLogin.load`, `ZkLogin.remove`. The signer itself is `Signer.zkLogin`.

### `ZkLoginSession` (const)

```ts
declare const ZkLoginSession: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly address: SuiAddress;
//     readonly maxEpoch: bigint;
//     readonly proof: {
//         readonly proofPoints: {
//             readonly a: readonly string[];
//             readonly b: readonly (readonly string[])[];
//             readonly c: readonly string[];
//         };
//         readonly issBase64Details: {
//             readonly value: string;
//             readonly indexMod4: number;
//         };
//         readonly headerBase64: string;
//         readonly addressSeed: string;
//     };
//     readonly expiresAt?: number | undefined;
// }
```

The public half of a zkLogin session: everything but the ephemeral secret.

- `address` is the zkLogin address. It is checked against the proof (its
  address seed and issuer) when a signer is made, legacy and current
  derivations both, so a session for another account is refused.
- `maxEpoch` is the last epoch the proof is accepted in, the bound the
  ephemeral key's nonce committed to.
- `expiresAt`, when known, is a wall-clock deadline in milliseconds since
  the Unix epoch (Enoki's `ZkLoginSession.expiresAt`). It is a cheap local
  approximation of `maxEpoch` the signer checks against `Clock`.

Nothing here is secret: the proof is public on chain in every signature the
session makes. The JWT is deliberately not part of it — a session that can
sign never needs the token again.

### `ZkLoginSignatureInputs` (const)

```ts
declare const ZkLoginSignatureInputs: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly proofPoints: {
//         readonly a: readonly string[];
//         readonly b: readonly (readonly string[])[];
//         readonly c: readonly string[];
//     };
//     readonly issBase64Details: {
//         readonly value: string;
//         readonly indexMod4: number;
//     };
//     readonly headerBase64: string;
//     readonly addressSeed: string;
// }
```

The proof inputs a zkLogin signature carries: the SDK's
`ZkLoginSignatureInputs` from `@mysten/sui/zklogin`, as a schema, so a
session can be stored and read back. Exactly what Enoki's
`createZkLoginZkp` and `EnokiFlow.getProof` answer.

### `ZkLoginSignerOptions` (interface)

```ts
export interface ZkLoginSignerOptions {
    readonly session: {
        readonly address: string;
        readonly maxEpoch: bigint | number | string;
        readonly proof: ZkLoginSignatureInputs;
        readonly expiresAt?: number;
    };
    readonly ephemeral: SdkSigner;
}
```

What `zkLogin` needs: the public session and the ephemeral signer.

`ephemeral` is any `@mysten/sui/cryptography` `Signer` — an
`Ed25519Keypair`, or a `WebCryptoSigner` over a non-extractable key held in
IndexedDB (what Enoki's `experimental_nativeCryptoSigner` stores). It is the
key the proof's nonce committed to; that commitment is inside the proof and
cannot be checked here, so a wrong ephemeral key produces signatures the node
refuses (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`, which `Tx.submit` reports as itself).

### `ZkLoginStoredSession` (const)

```ts
declare const ZkLoginStoredSession: Schema
// decodes to:
// {
//     readonly session: {
//         readonly address: SuiAddress;
//         readonly maxEpoch: bigint;
//         readonly proof: {
//             readonly proofPoints: {
//                 readonly a: readonly string[];
//                 readonly b: readonly (readonly string[])[];
//                 readonly c: readonly string[];
//             };
//             readonly issBase64Details: {
//                 readonly value: string;
//                 readonly indexMod4: number;
//             };
//             readonly headerBase64: string;
//             readonly addressSeed: string;
//         };
//         readonly expiresAt?: number | undefined;
//     };
//     readonly ephemeral: {
//         readonly scheme: "ED25519";
//         readonly sealed: string;
//     };
// }
```

One stored zkLogin session: the public session and the sealed ephemeral
secret (an Ed25519 32-byte seed, sealed by a `SecretSealer`). The JSON
a `save` writes under its key.

### `attach` (const)

```ts
declare const attach: (target: Built | Signed, signature: string) => Effect.Effect<Signed, TransportError | SigningError, Sui>
```

Attaches a signature somebody else made — a sender's wallet, a remote
sponsor, an HSM — to built or already signed bytes, after checking it.

The signature is verified against the bytes (`Verify.verifyTransactionSignature`,
so a zkLogin or multisig signature is checked through the node), and the
address it was made by must be the sender or the gas owner the bytes name.
A `Built` becomes a `Signed` carrying that one signature; a `Signed` gains it.
The same signature attached twice is attached once.

```ts
const built = yield* Tx.fromBytes(senderBytes)
const withSender = yield* Tx.attach(built, senderSignature)
const signed = yield* Tx.cosign(withSender, sponsor)
```

**Fails with: `SigningError` (the signature is empty, does not parse, is not valid for these bytes, or was made by an address the bytes will not accept), `TransportError` (a zkLogin or multisig check could not reach a usable answer).**

### `build` (const)

```ts
declare const build: (input: Transaction | Recipe, opts: {
    readonly sender: SuiAddress;
    readonly gasOwner?: SuiAddress;
    /**
     * The last epoch the transaction may be valid in: a signer's session
     * bound (`Signer.maxEpoch`). The default expiration's `maxEpoch` is capped
     * at it, and a bound already below the current epoch fails the build
     * before anything resolves.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    readonly maxEpoch?: bigint;
}) => Effect.Effect<Built, TransportError | SimulationFailed | BuildError | PolicyDenied, Sui>
```

Builds a transaction into signable bytes.

**Building always simulates, on every transport.** When the SDK's resolver
simulates with checks enabled, an execution failure there arrives as
`SimulationFailed` and nothing extra is spent. It does not always: the gRPC
plugin simulates whenever it runs, but the core resolver every other
transport uses (JSON-RPC, GraphQL, a custom client) simulates only to choose
a gas budget it was not given, and neither runs at all for a transaction
that was **already fully resolved** (every input resolved, gas price,
budget and payment set). `Tx.build` decides which case it is in against the
client that resolves, before the build, and runs one explicit
`simulateTransaction` with checks enabled whenever the SDK will not — so a
recipe that presets `tx.setGasBudget(...)` on a non-gRPC transport is still
shown to execute, and a Move abort is `SimulationFailed` with no gas charged
rather than `ExecutionFailed` after a send.

An interrupted build cancels the request it started: the SDK is handed a
client whose Core calls carry the Effect's `AbortSignal`.

The resolver's own reads go straight to the SDK rather than through
`SuiCore`'s read retries, so `Tx.build` retries the whole resolve on a
retryable `TransportError` with the same policy (five attempts, jittered
exponential backoff capped at ten seconds). Nothing has been signed, so a
retry cannot send anything twice.

When the recipe set no expiration, `SubmitConfig.expiration` decides one.
The default, `ValidDuring`, bounds the transaction to the **current epoch and
the next**, names the chain (bytes signed for testnet cannot land on mainnet)
and carries a `u32` nonce from `SubmitConfig.nonce`. There is **no
wall-clock bound** unless `SubmitConfig.validFor` asks for one: no Sui
network accepts a timestamp expiration yet, and a node refuses any
transaction carrying one. The expiration that ends up in the bytes is
recorded on the result, because it is what `Tx.reconcile` needs later to
prove a transaction can no longer land.

`Tx.build` takes no sender lock; `Tx.run` is what holds one around every
build (and through submit when the bytes pin anything). Called on its own,
two concurrent builds for one address can pick the same gas coin.

**A refusal from the resolver is a `BuildError`.** The SDK resolves inputs
and gas through the node, and a node that refuses the transaction itself —
a function that does not exist, an object owned by another address, gas
selection with nothing to select (gRPC `INVALID_ARGUMENT` or
`FAILED_PRECONDITION`), an input it cannot find (`NOT_FOUND`) — is reported
with the node's message, the `TransportError` kept as the cause; so is the
SDK resolver's own refusal ("Insufficient balance of <coin> for owner
<address>"). A `TransportError` that remains is the network, named
`"Tx.build"`.

**Gas comes from the address balance by default.** A payment the recipe
left unset is the resolver's choice, and every SDK resolver pays from the
payer's SUI address balance when it covers the budget and the transaction
does not use `tx.gas`, and from coins otherwise; the span attribute
`sui.gas_payment` says which (`"addressBalance"`, `"coinReservation"`,
`"coins"`). Under `SubmitConfig.gasPayment: "requireAddressBalance"` an unset
payment is pinned to the balance before the build (unless the transaction
uses `tx.gas`), and coins chosen for a `tx.gas` user fail the build.

**Gas from an address balance is checked before anything is signed.** When
the gas payment is an explicit empty list (what `Tx.sponsored` sets, or the
`"requireAddressBalance"` policy), the payer — the gas owner, or the sender —
must hold at least the gas budget plus the SUI the bytes withdraw from it in
its SUI address balance, coins not counted; the simulation does not check it
and the node refuses the bytes at execution. That costs one `getBalance`
read, for those transactions only. Bytes paying from an address balance
with no address-owned input need a `ValidDuring` expiration, which a node
otherwise refuses outright (the default expiration is one), and must not use
`tx.gas`: with an empty payment the gas coin is empty, which a simulation
does not show and execution fails on, charging gas.

`Built.gasOwner` is read out of the bytes: it is present whenever the bytes
name a gas owner other than the sender, whether `opts.gasOwner` or the
recipe set it. So is `Built.gas` (0.5.0): budget, price, payer, payment refs
and their `source` — `"addressBalance"`, `"coinReservation"` or `"coins"`,
the `sui.gas_payment` attribute. `Built.simulation` (0.5.0) is the explicit
simulate above when it ran, which is a simulation of exactly the returned
bytes; when the SDK's resolver simulated instead (always on gRPC) it
simulated the transaction before its budget was set, and there is none.

**`opts.maxEpoch` keeps the window inside a session** (0.5.0): the default
expiration's `maxEpoch` is the current epoch plus one or the bound,
whichever is lower, and a bound below the current epoch fails before
anything resolves. `Tx.run` passes the signer's (and sponsor's)
`Signer.maxEpoch` here. A recipe's own expiration is the recipe's choice
and is not changed.

**Fails with: `BuildError` (the recipe threw, an input could not be resolved, the session bound `opts.maxEpoch` is below the current epoch — its `cause` is the `SigningError { reason: "expired" }` `Tx.run` reports instead, the node refused to resolve the transaction, a zero-gas requirement was not met, the payer's address balance cannot cover the budget and withdrawals, coins were chosen under `gasPayment: "requireAddressBalance"`, or an address-balance payment lacks an epoch-bounded expiration or is used with `tx.gas`), `SimulationFailed` (the transaction would abort on chain), `PolicyDenied` (rule `"maxGasBudget"`: the budget the node chose is over `SubmitConfig.maxGasBudget`), `TransportError`.**

### `chainOf` (const)

Re-exported from `@unconfirmed/sui-effect`.

### `checkClaims` (const)

```ts
declare const checkClaims: (intent: Intent, simulation: Simulation, parties: Parties) => ReadonlyArray<ClaimViolation>
```

Compares the simulated effects with the claims, exactly and in code.

Deny by default: a coin the sender loses needs an outflow claim that covers
the amount (net of gas), an address that gains a coin or an object needs to
be a claimed recipient, and a non-coin object that leaves the sender —
transferred, shared, wrapped or deleted — needs to be a claimed object.

**Gas is gas wherever it is paid from.** A node's balance changes cover coin
objects and address balances alike, so the payer's SUI delta carries the
gas whether the payment was a gas coin or the payer's address balance (an
empty payment), and it is taken back out either way. Anything else that
leaves the sender's address balance — a `balance::send_funds` of a
`tx.balance()` withdrawal — is an outflow like any coin transfer, and its
receiver a recipient. Accumulator writes are bookkeeping and never an object.

An empty answer means the claims held.

**Never fails.**

### `cosign` (const)

```ts
declare const cosign: (signed: Signed, signer: Signer) => Effect.Effect<Signed, SigningError, never>
```

Adds one more signature to already signed bytes, for a sponsored or
multi-party transaction. The bytes are untouched, so both parties sign
exactly the same transaction.

As in `sign`, the co-signer's address must be the sender or the gas
owner named in the bytes.

**Fails with: `SigningError`.**

### `describeClaims` (const)

```ts
declare const describeClaims: (intent: Intent) => ReadonlyArray<string>
```

The claims as sentences, for the edge between the summary and the claims: a
harmless sentence must not sit in front of permissions it has no use for.

Answers with no lines when the intent claims nothing.

**Never fails.**

### `describeEffects` (const)

```ts
declare const describeEffects: (simulation: Simulation, parties: Parties) => ReadonlyArray<string>
```

The simulated effects as sentences a semantic screen can read: addresses
become labels, types lose their package ids, and only SUI carries a number.
What it cannot say reliably it does not say.

Always answers with at least one line.

**Never fails.**

### `ephemeral` (const)

```ts
declare const ephemeral: Effect.Effect<Signer>
```

A fresh Ed25519 credential that exists only for this process. For tests,
localnet and throwaway addresses.

This is the one place sui-effect does not take randomness from Effect's
`Random`: key generation must come from a cryptographically secure source,
and `Random` is a seeded, test-controllable PRNG whose whole purpose is to be
reproducible. `new Ed25519Keypair()` uses the SDK's CSPRNG (`@noble/curves`
over `crypto.getRandomValues`). A `TestClock`-style deterministic key would
be a security bug, not a convenience.

**Never fails.**

### `fold` (const)

```ts
declare const fold: (batch: FoldBatch) => Recipe
```

The recipe for one fold transaction: merge the batch's coins and deposit
them into the owner's address balance with `0x2::coin::send_funds<T>`.

- `gas: "foldedCoins"` (SUI): the first 256 coins are the gas payment, the
  rest are merged into the gas coin, and the gas coin itself is deposited —
  no SUI coin is left.
- `gas: "addressBalance"`: the coins are merged into the first and it is
  deposited; the payment is `[]`, so gas comes from the SUI address balance,
  which `Tx.build` checks covers the budget.
- `gas: "auto"`: the same, with the payment left to the resolver.

The coins are pinned at the versions the plan read (`tx.objectRef`), so a
coin that moved since fails the build rather than folding something else.

**Never fails; the recipe throws for an empty batch, more than 500 coins, or `"foldedCoins"` on a coin type that is not SUI, which `Tx.build` reports as `BuildError`.**

### `foldCoins` (const)

```ts
declare const foldCoins: <E = never>(opts: {
    readonly signer: Signer;
    /** One coin type to fold; every coin type the signer holds coins of when omitted. */
    readonly coinType?: CoinType;
    /** At most this many coins per transaction: 1 to 500, 500 by default. */
    readonly maxCoinsPerTransaction?: number;
    /**
     * A plan from `Tx.planFold`, run as given rather than re-planned.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    readonly plan?: FoldPlan;
    /**
     * `Tx.run`'s `approve`, per batch, with the batch it is about to fold.
     *
     * @since 0.5.0
     */
    readonly approve?: (prepared: Prepared, batch: FoldBatch) => Effect.Effect<void, E>;
}) => Effect.Effect<FoldResult, RunError | E, Sui>
```

Folds the signer's coin objects into its address balance: `planFold`
for `signer.address`, then one `run` of `fold` per batch, in
order — SUI first, paying for its own fold, so a fresh account whose SUI is
all coins needs no address balance to start. Afterwards gas comes from the
balance, and transactions that pin nothing else and do not use `tx.gas` run
without holding the sender lock past their build (one that uses `tx.gas` is
paid with a reservation of the whole balance, which holds it).

Each batch pins its coins, so it runs under the sender lock. It stops at the
first batch that fails, with that failure; the batches before it executed and
stay executed. Calling it again re-plans from what is left, so it is safe to
repeat. Dust that cannot pay for its own fold is in `plan.skipped`, and an
owner with no coins answers an empty plan with nothing executed.

**A previewed plan is run as given** (0.5.0). Pass the `plan` a person was
shown and it is not re-planned — `coinType` and `maxCoinsPerTransaction` are
then ignored — so what runs is exactly what was previewed; a coin in it that
has moved since fails that batch's build with `BuildError`, as any pinned
input does. A plan for another owner is a `BuildError` before anything
runs. `approve` is `Tx.run`'s, once per batch, shown the batch beside the
prepared transaction: `foldMovement` of the two is what the fold
moves from coins into the balance, which the simulation's balance changes
alone do not show.

**Fails with: `BuildError`, `SimulationFailed`, `PolicyDenied`, `SigningError`, `ExecutionFailed`, `NotApplied`, `SubmissionUnknown`, `JournalError`, `TransportError` — exactly `run`'s, for the batch that failed — and `E`, whatever `approve` fails with.**

### `foldMovement` (const)

```ts
declare const foldMovement: (batch: FoldBatch, effects: Simulation | Executed) => FoldMovement
```

The coin → address-balance movement of one fold, which its balance changes
do not show: a node nets them per address, so a fold of 1,000 SUI of coins
reports only the gas (`+3,939,506` MIST on localnet, protocol 139, for five
faucet coins whose storage rebate beat the gas), and the coins' balances are
not in the effects at all — the coins are simply deleted, and the deposit is
an accumulator write with no amount on it. So the movement is computed from
what is known exactly: the batch's coins were pinned at the versions the plan
read (`tx.objectRef`), so their balances are the plan's; the effects say
which of them were consumed; and the owner's net balance change is the rest.

For `simulation` pass `prepared.simulation` (`Tx.prepare(Tx.fold(batch),
{ sender })`, or what `Tx.foldCoins`' `approve` is shown), or the
`Executed` the fold produced.

**Never fails.**

### `fromBytes` (const)

```ts
declare const fromBytes: (bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>) => Effect.Effect<Built, BuildError, Sui>
```

Turns transaction bytes that came from somewhere else into a `Built`,
reading everything the lifecycle needs out of the bytes themselves.

This is how a stateless sponsor, a wallet bridge or a service that was
handed someone's transaction gets back into the lifecycle: `sign`, `attach`,
`cosign` and `submit` all take what this returns. The sender, the gas owner
and the expiration are read out of the bytes, and the digest is computed from
them, so nothing is taken on trust. **Never build a `Built` by hand**: one
that leaves out `expiration` or `chain` silently disables `Tx.reconcile`'s
expiry rule and chain guard.

`chain` is the chain of the `Sui` in context. Bytes whose expiration names a
chain (the default `ValidDuring` does) must name that one; bytes whose
expiration names none (`Epoch`, `None`) cannot be checked, and are taken to
be for this chain.

`gas` is read out of the bytes too (budget, price, payer, payment and its
source); there is never a `simulation`.

Unlike `Tx.build`, nothing is simulated: `Tx.prepare(built, { sender })`
simulates the bytes (with `preflight` and an intent) and shows them to an
`approve` step, or run `sui.simulate(built.bytes)` before co-signing bytes
you did not build.

**Fails with: `BuildError` (the bytes are not a full transaction this SDK can read, name no sender or no gas data, or are bound to another chain).**

### `fromConfig` (const)

```ts
declare const fromConfig: (name?: string) => Effect.Effect<Signer, Config.ConfigError>
```

Reads a secret key from configuration and builds its signer.

Two spellings, told apart by the text itself:

- a **Bech32 `suiprivkey1…`**, whose flag names one of the four schemes (`ED25519`, `Secp256k1`, `Secp256r1`, `MLDSA65`);
- a **32-byte hex seed** (64 hex characters, `0x` optional), which carries no
  scheme and is read as **Ed25519** — the default every Sui tool uses for a
  raw seed. This is what a secret manager or another language's SDK hands
  over, and reading it here is what keeps `fromHex` and the decoded bytes out
  of application code.

The key is read with `Config.Redacted`, which is wiped
(`Redacted.wipeUnsafe`) as soon as it has been decoded, and the decoded
bytes never leave this function. Neither does anything derived from them: the failure carries
one fixed sentence and no `cause`, because the decoder's own message quotes
the input it rejected.

**Fails with: `ConfigError` when the variable is missing, is neither spelling, or names a scheme that has no keypair class (`MultiSig`, `ZkLogin`, `Passkey` — use `remote` for those).**

### `fromKeypair` (const)

```ts
declare const fromKeypair: (keypair: Keypair) => Signer
```

`fromSdkSigner` under the name the spec gave it when a keypair was the
only thing it took. A `Keypair` **is** an SDK `Signer`, so this is a thin
alias kept for callers who hold one.

**Never fails.**

### `fromMnemonic` (const)

```ts
declare const fromMnemonic: (phrase: Redacted.Redacted<string> | string, options?: FromMnemonicOptions) => Effect.Effect<LockableSigner, KeyError>
```

A signer derived from a BIP-39 recovery phrase, **checked first**: the word
count (12, 15, 18, 21 or 24), every word in the English list, and the
checksum. The SDK's `deriveKeypair` checks none of them, so a phrase with one
word wrong derives a valid account nobody has ever funded; here it is a
`KeyError { reason: "invalidMnemonic" }` and nothing is derived. The phrase
is normalized (trimmed, lowercased, single-spaced) before the check; no
BIP-39 passphrase is used, which is what every Sui wallet does.

Paths are the standard Sui ones (`Signer.derivationPath`):
`m/44'/784'/{account}'/0'/0'` (Ed25519, the default),
`m/54'/784'/{account}'/0/0` (Secp256k1), `m/74'/784'/{account}'/0/0`
(Secp256r1). An explicit `path` must have its scheme's shape (the SDK's
`isValidHardenedPath` / `isValidBIP32Path`, with the scheme's own purpose)
and every level below 2^31.

The answer is a `LockableSigner`: it holds the derived key in a buffer
nobody else references, and `lock` zeroes it. The BIP-39 seed and the HD
nodes are zeroed before this returns. A `Redacted` phrase is read, not
wiped: it is the caller's (`Redacted.wipeUnsafe` it when done).

**Fails with: `KeyError` (`invalidMnemonic`, `invalidPath`, `unsupportedScheme`). The message names a bad word's position, never a word.**

### `fromSdkSigner` (const)

```ts
declare const fromSdkSigner: (keypair: SdkSigner) => Signer
```

Wraps any `@mysten/sui/cryptography` `Signer`.

The SDK's `Signer` is the base class every credential extends: `Keypair` and
its four schemes, but also a Ledger signer, a wallet adapter's signer, a KMS
signer — anything that can `toSuiAddress`, `getKeyScheme`, `signTransaction`
and `signPersonalMessage`. Nothing here needs the secret, so nothing here
needs a keypair, and the `Signer` this returns exposes no secret material
either.

**`toSuiAddress()` and `getKeyScheme()` are read here, synchronously**, and
the address and scheme of the returned `Signer` are whatever they answered at
this moment: a credential that changes accounts later is a different
`Signer`, built again. A test double therefore needs both of those methods,
not only `signTransaction` — a double without `getKeyScheme` used to produce
`scheme: undefined` and nothing complained until a validator did.

**Clear-signing inputs are the SDK signer's own concern.** The Ledger signer
takes `signTransaction(bytes, bcsObjects?, resolution?)` and resolves those
extra arguments through the client it was constructed with; this passes only
the bytes, which is the whole of the base `Signer` contract. A device that
needs more than the bytes gets it from its own client, or from
`remote`.

For a credential that is not an SDK `Signer` at all — a remote service, a
hardware device behind your own protocol — use `remote`, which takes
Effects and the address to sign as.

**Throws** a `TypeError` naming the missing member when the argument is not
an SDK signer — that is a wiring mistake in the caller, not a runtime
failure a program recovers from.

**Otherwise never fails: a bad address or signature surfaces as a `SigningError` from the member that produced it, not from construction.**

### `fromSecretKey` (const)

```ts
declare const fromSecretKey: (secret: Redacted.Redacted<string> | string | Uint8Array, options?: FromSecretKeyOptions) => Effect.Effect<LockableSigner, KeyError>
```

A signer over an imported secret key: a Bech32 `suiprivkey1…` (its flag
names the scheme; a contradicting `scheme` option is refused), a 32-byte hex
seed (`0x` optional), or 32 raw bytes — the last two read as
`options.scheme`, default `ED25519`.

**The bytes are copied before any keypair is built over them.** The SDK's
Secp256k1/Secp256r1 keypairs keep a reference to the array they are given,
so building over the caller's buffer would let a later write to it change
the key, and zeroing it break the signer. Here the caller's `Uint8Array` is
copied and left untouched, decoded bytes are zeroed once copied, and the
answer is a `LockableSigner` whose `lock` zeroes the copy. A
`Redacted` input is read, not wiped.

**Fails with: `KeyError` (`invalidSecretKey` — the message is fixed and never quotes the input — or `unsupportedScheme` for `MLDSA65`, `ZkLogin`, … and a contradicting `scheme`).**

### `gaslessStablecoinTransfer` (const)

```ts
declare const gaslessStablecoinTransfer: (opts: {
    readonly coinType: CoinType;
    readonly amount: bigint;
    readonly recipient: SuiAddress;
    /**
     * Fail the build unless the resolved gas price and budget are both zero.
     *
     * @since 0.4.0
     */
    readonly requireZeroGas?: boolean;
}) => Recipe
```

Builds the canonical transfer shape that an eligible stablecoin can execute
without SUI gas: a `Balance<T>` from the SDK's `Transaction#balance` intent,
consumed by `0x2::balance::send_funds<T>`.

The recipe deliberately does **not** set gas price, budget or payment. The
gRPC and GraphQL SDK resolvers simulate the transaction, check the network's
current protocol configuration and set zero gas only when the coin type,
amount and resolved PTB qualify. It also does not hardcode an allowlist or
the current 0.01 minimum: protocol configuration stores both eligibility and
each token's minimum in base units, while this API has no trustworthy token
decimals. `Tx.build`'s mandatory simulation therefore remains the authority;
a UI that already has verified coin metadata may reject a too-small display
amount earlier.

This sends to the recipient's address balance. The SDK may source the amount
from the sender's address balance, coin objects, or both, and resolves the
required eligible helper calls itself.

**`requireZeroGas: true`** makes "gasless" a requirement rather than a hope.
Without it, a coin type or amount that does not qualify — or a transport
that does not detect eligibility, such as JSON-RPC — still builds, and the
sender quietly pays SUI gas. With it, the recipe installs a build plugin
that checks what the resolver chose, and `Tx.build` fails with `BuildError`
unless the gas price and the gas budget are both zero.

**Never fails; the recipe it returns can throw only if the SDK rejects an input, which `Tx.build` reports as `BuildError` before anything is signed.**

### `isUnresolved` (const)

```ts

    readonly _tag: "Unknown";
```

Whether this entry is still waiting for an answer, and therefore something
